## End of Hostilities?

2022-03-01 · Formula for Peace

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/df552dfd-3be6-4f06-aae5-4fc4c7edbe57_1050x1317.jpeg)

*There has been some chatter, mostly in Russian billionaire circles, about different people volunteering to help broker a peace agreement between Russia and Ukraine. Practicing philosopher on March 1 in his Echo Moscow blog [offers](https://echo.msk.ru/blog/pastuhov_v/2987663-echo/) (at least what I think is) sane advice. Here’s a quick xlation:*

1. All the experience I have accumulated over almost forty years of work in the field of conflict resolution suggests that the success of any complex negotiation does not depend on the skill of the negotiators, their good or bad will, but on the presence or absence of a negotiating formula and negotiating position. If there are none, if before the start of negotiations at least one of the parties has not formulated a position that allows for the existence of points of contact, the negotiations are meaningless even if the mediator is the Pope or the Chief Rabbi.
2. The second condition for successful negotiations is realism. In the current situation, realism presupposes an honest and courageous recognition of the fact that there is no negotiating formula for making peace between Russia and Ukraine today. It doesn't exist in principle. That is, if Crimea, Donbas, the neutral status of Ukraine, restitution, militarization, compensation, satisfaction and so on become the subject of discussion, negotiations can be curtailed immediately, because they will yield zero results. On all these issues, Russia and Ukraine, and more broadly, Russia and the West, are now unable to agree.
3. The third indispensable condition for conducting any negotiations, with the exception of negotiations on complete and unconditional surrender, is to give both parties the opportunity to save face. This is triple important when it comes to negotiations between belligerent countries, where governments are backed by enraged masses warmed by bloodshed. The point is no longer what Putin and Zelensky can agree on, but what the masses behind them can accept. Putin depends on the mood of the masses just as much as Zelensky, just the format is different. Nothing that goes against the expectations of the masses, neither one nor the other can sign. We have already seen this in Paris.
4. Based on these three conditions, the negotiation formula looks like a very narrow isthmus with an almost illusory possibility to walk on water “like on dry land.” Speech can and should be exclusively about the conditions of a truce, and not peace. This is the only more or less working window of opportunity. Stabilization of the situation here and now without even trying to give a hint about in what direction, how and how quickly this conflict should be resolved. The limit of the possible is an armistice agreement, like with Japan, leaving all questions open, but allowing somehow to streamline humanitarian problems.
5. The only point of discussion and, accordingly, the point of contact today can and should be humanitarian issues. Cessation of new killings, exchange of prisoners and corpses. The only way to save face is a tear on the cheek, even if it's a crocodile tear. Crocodiles also have children and even grandchildren. Leaving the humanitarian zone means the death of the negotiation process. There can be only one general thesis now - any ideas and ambitions, grievances and claims cannot be an excuse for the nightmare into which it all resulted.
6. From a practical point of view, the base can be agreements on a ceasefire and the withdrawal of Russian troops to positions before February 24, with the exception of the territories of Russia-occupied areas of Donetsk and Luhansk regions, which Russia unilaterally recognized as independent states and which in any case were not controlled by Ukraine until February 24. Everything else should be left for later. This is in the interests of Ukraine, which needs to save the lives of its citizens, and this does not contradict Putin’s concept of “fraternal peoples”: South and North Korea are generally one people, but they can only exist on opposite sides of the barbed wire.

## Day 7

2022-03-02 · Giant pink blob

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/75672463-b73b-4c60-803a-a7f47d74c257_1096x731.png)

So, that pink blob pouring down from southern Belarus towards Kyiv should be our main concern, according to the [Institute for the Study of War](https://www.understandingwar.org/).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/96d2255c-2c5b-4bd3-b919-a2cd0bd005b1_642x163.png)

The New York Times publishes this up-to-date ISW [invasion map](https://www.nytimes.com/interactive/2022/world/europe/ukraine-maps.html):

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/bc031a49-51d5-42c6-a7ab-7eb9a505e68a_494x551.png)

And we have another NYT [article](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/03/01/world/europe/russian-convoy-ukraine.html) talking about the pink blob citing ISW, as well as the 40-kilometer Russian military convoy that’s within it.

I became a little leery of ISW after they published a [loopy counterfactual](https://www.understandingwar.org/sites/default/files/Ukraine%20Invasion%20Forecast%20Series%20Part%201%20ISW%20CT%20December%202021.pdf?fbclid=IwAR2XMXeaIPep1VRBXDubW-c3IKBPGNV9mmfL-hTJvVQ8wdEZVgDiItLb2hE) in December 2021. The “[bounded rationality](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/day-5?s=w)” bullshit threw me.

For what it’s worth, [General Sir Richard Barons](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/03/01/world/europe/russian-convoy-ukraine.html) has chimed in for [Sky News](https://news.sky.com/story/ukraine-invasion-40-mile-russian-military-convoy-nearing-kyiv-satellite-pictures-show-12554623):

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/d1bc0737-87f6-49a5-8ad6-6f6da74f6b0b_688x291.png)

And the Washington Post [says](https://www.washingtonpost.com/national-security/2022/03/01/ukraine-convoy-stalled-heavy-fighting/?itid=hp-banner-low) the convoy has stalled.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/25ee4a99-b39b-462b-a78a-75632a8c76c4_693x270.jpeg)

The orcs in the Russian convoy represented by the giant pink blob don’t appear to be motivated or capable fighters. If they attempt to enter Kyiv, they will be vaporized. And If they fan out and encircle the capital in the coming weeks, we’ll hunt them down and turn them into fertilizer.

So much for the giant pink blob.

## Day 9

2022-03-04 · Battle for Borodyanka

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/96b84334-507b-4269-8d12-e640e0867517_1920x1454.jpeg)  
*Photo c/o Dmytro Kuleba , c/o Reuters...*

###### 

Borodyanka, sometimes spelled Borodianka, is an urban-type settlement in Kyiv region and the administrative center of its eponymous district. Population: 13,044, about 1,000 more than 20 years ago.

The town is located on the river Zdvyzh which is a tributary of the Teteriv River [at the end](https://www.floodmap.net/?gi=711635) of the Polesia flood basin. It’s about 40 kilometers northwest of Kyiv, depending on what route you take.

I’ve bicycled there numerous times. Terrain: **flat as a pancake**.

They grow a lot of of pot in Borodyanka, so I’ve been following closely the battle there since [Day 1](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/russias-further-invasion-day-1?s=w), occasionally posting videos on of the mess at @[dronedept](https://twitter.com/dronedept/status/1498790785210372103) (while being careful not to divulge critical time-sensitive info about the secret plantation and frantic efforts to dislodge the orcs that rolled in on February 24).

There’s a [little airport](https://www.google.com/maps/@50.662565,29.9329307,3a,75y,222.15h,40.96t/data=!3m8!1e1!3m6!1sAF1QipPgS5q_eU8JTkGxbPJM1G_PloGd7EYZQnn49O1W!2e10!3e11!6shttps:%2F%2Flh5.googleusercontent.com%2Fp%2FAF1QipPgS5q_eU8JTkGxbPJM1G_PloGd7EYZQnn49O1W%3Dw203-h100-k-no-pi0-ya358.3119-ro-0-fo100!7i13200!8i6600) in Borodyanka, but that’s [another story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borodianka_(air_base)).

About 200 residents of Borodyanka [were evacuated yesterday](https://kyiv.tsn.ua/iz-zruynovanoyi-okupantami-borodyanki-evakuyuvali-200-lyudey-yakiy-viglyad-maye-selische-video-1995544.html). Most of them lived in the center of town, which, as you can see in the video below, was blown up into tiny bits.

My friends there live about four kilometers from the center of town, where there are lots of little dams and levees to control the spring floods and irrigate the surrounding whatever-they-grow-in-those fields.

To make a long story short, the orcs invaded Borodyanka, got shot up, left Borodyanka, got shot up, came back to Borodyanka, got shot up, and then were herded by Ukranian defense forces into a bunch of boggy fields and turned into fertilizer for the secret pot plantation.

It looked like a happy ending, until the orcs re-invaded and took the town’s remaining residents hostage. Then Russian planes dropped bombs on the town. A sad tale to to ponder the next time you light up a joint and daydream about *denazification*.

## Day 10

2022-03-05 · Simplifying public-facing nouns

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/acc272d6-1073-4312-af1f-b11fa8cfc1a7_800x663.jpeg)  
*Testing out flight helmets, c/o Old Photos of the World*

###### 

*Lots of nerdy military updates w/ maps about the mess on Substack. [Here’s one](https://bit.ly/3HHWvY0), if you’re interested. And [this one](https://www.facebook.com/stefan.korshak) on Facebook w/ links to many others.*

This is prob the best map I’ve come across, if you don’t want to read about it.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/4eecde42-cae5-488d-89b8-c2500c9f1fb5_1280x933.jpeg)

\*\*\*

Upon recovery from a bit of cred squidginess following yesterday’s gush over the strafing of a nuclear power plant in Zapori***zhzh***ia**,** it occurs to me that, somewhere in the gaping void that is understanding Russia’s further invasion, there lie previously ignored linguistic possibilities of potential benefit to all.

Despite their ever-increasing presence in everyday life, weirdly rendered place names and surnames are most annoying. And they are notoriously inaccessible to monolingual laypeople who rely on Chuck at CBS and his low-frequency off camera stand-up jargon, i.e.:

> # What the hell was that?

How would Chuck pronounce *Vydubychi* or *Kropyvnytskyi* or *Pa-lya-ny-tsia?*

When it comes to written news reports, a corpus analysis (n ≈ 1 zillion words) reveals that Russia-invasion news reports contain startlingly high proportions of certain difficult-to-process impenetrable features – including extra hard-to-pronounce letter combinations and weird-looking first- and last name suffixes relative to baseline genres of written and spoken English.

It is at best linguistically irresponsible – at worst it is stupid journalistically – to simply ignore, mechanically transcribe and/or copy + paste letter combination features that are recalled and comprehended at lower rates than excerpts without these features, even for experienced experts.

So, edit problematic features out. Just spell it. This is war, for Chrissake, not a classroom at NYU. Use Google Maps for place names.

Conserve letters: **Zaporizhia**. There’s no good reason for double up “***zh***s.” Don’t repeat “***y***s” in surnames, except Zelensk***yy*** — but not Zelensk***iy***, Zelensk***yi***. Maybe just write Ze, or Z. The same goes for “***i***s,” as in *[Iul](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/weightless-self-promotion?s=w)**[iia](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/weightless-self-promotion?s=w)** [[Mendel]](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/weightless-self-promotion?s=w)*. Her name is painful to look at, much less pronounce correctly, or even think about otherwise.

## Day 11

2022-03-06 · Tickling Russia with sanctions

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/182dcabb-f88a-4ea2-8754-de7dc1f4ea18_493x329.jpeg)

###### 

**It goes like this:**

1. Putin's main mistake was that he was sure Ukrainians would not fight.
2. Ukraine’s main mistake was thinking the West would fight.

The West’s main mistake is thinking it would not have to fight after Putin’s war spread to NATO-member states.

***Putin doesn’t eat from hunger. He eats from fear, and fear has large eyes.***

There are plenty of reasons for Putin to be terrified, if you believe the disgruntled analyst at the Federal Security Service of the Russian Federation. His/her grumpy letter was [posted](https://www.facebook.com/vladimir.osechkin/posts/4811633942268327) on March 4 by Vladimir Osechkin to Facebook.

Some excerpts:

> I can't tell you what the top people were thinking when they approved the special military operation, but now they are methodically siccing all the dogs on us [in the FSB].

The author says he/she hasn’t been getting a lot of sleep since Putin signed off on the special military operation, which has opened “a Pandora’s box of problems, including global famine, since Russia and Ukraine are the world’s biggest grain suppliers.”

> This year's harvest will be smaller, and logistical problems will make the catastrophe more acute.

Whoever wrote the letter says his/her *speciality* is analysis and complains that top brass [in the Kremlin] have increased pressure to customize reports as per their requirements.

> You need to write in a victorious style so that there are no questions.

He/she says it’s likely Central Bank boss Elvira Nabullina and her assistants will blamed for not doing enough to mitigate damage from sanctions to what’s left of the country’s economy.

> No one knew that there would be such a war, so no one prepared for such sanctions.

Lots of bitching about Ramzan Kadyrov’s self-aggrandizing role in Russia’s botched invasion of Ukraine.

> [The Chechens] were simply torn to pieces in some places. Someone spread the rumor that it was the FSB that leaked the itinerary of Chechen units to the Ukrainians. I do not have such information.

According to the letter, the blitzkrieg was poorly planned and has failed miserably.

> It is simply impossible to complete the task now: Even if Zelensky and the authorities had been captured in the first 1-3 days and all the key buildings in Kyiv had been seized…. and surrendered … What next? …With whom to negotiate?

The author says leaders of Ukraine’s pro-Russia Opposition Platform - For Life party have be less than helpful.

> [Viktor] Medvedchuk is a coward, he fled. [Yuriy] Boiko refuses to work with us. They wanted to bring in [Oleh] Tsarev, but even our pro-Russia allies were against this. Bring back [disgraced ex-President Viktor] Yanukovych?

Whoever wrote the letter says it will be impossible to get enough Russians to occupy, must less manage, a vanquished Ukraine.

> Even with minimal resistance, we need 500,000 or more occupiers. And that’s not counting the people who will put in charge of managing the supply system.

As for KIAs and MIAs, the picture is murky.

> Casualties: I don't know how many there are. No one knows. There was command and control during first two days of the specop, but now no one has a clue about what’s going on. KIAs are definitely in the thousands. Maybe 10,000, maybe 5,000 or maybe only 2,000.

Other interesting bits from the bitchy letter:

> *Denazification* and *demilitarization* are not analytical categories, because they do not have clearly defined parameters by which one can determine the level of accomplishment or non-completion of the task.

> Our Z emblem will be compared with Hitler’s swastika.

> Kadyrov is mouthing off for a reason… He wants to burnish his image as someone who is influential and invincible.

The last part of the letter talks about how Russia’s further invasion of Ukraine will overload Russia’s already overburdened prison system, touches on whether Putin will press the red button “to destroy the world,” and questions the readiness [of Putin’s disciples] to sacrifice themselves for someone who has isolated himself from his cheerleaders in the Russian Federation Council and top advisers.

I listened to [Feygin Live](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MWkNry5BMRo) w/ Illarionov and Latynina’s [chit chat](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J5dn_dPXuIY) with Podoliak. Glad to hear both guests say “**I made a mistake”** by not believing months of Tiger Team warnings about Putin’s intention to further invade Ukraine. Admitting fuck-ups makes it less likely they will repeat them. Same goes for Fred and Kimberly at the [Institute for the Study of War](https://www.understandingwar.org/), which posts an [update](https://www.understandingwar.org/backgrounder/ukraine-conflict-update-16) on the current tactical situation. ISF analysts are probably hoping everyone has forgotten their counterfactual “bounds of Putin’s rationality” [bullshit](https://www.understandingwar.org/report/putins-likely-course-action-ukraine-part-1).

---

Practicing London-based philosopher Vladimir Pastukhov has written another [pragmatic-sounding article](https://poligon.media/putin-svinstvo) about the current mess. Find my quick xlation of it below.

\*\*\*

**Apocalyptic Scenarios: Putin prepares an “act of savagery,” but is keeping an eye on Belovezhskaya Pushcha**

> Knowing Putin, his words “everything is going according to plan” should be read “everything went wrong,” the words “we will not use conscripts” should be understood as “we are already using conscripts, forcing them to sign a contract by force before being sent into battle” and the words “we are not going to introduce martial law and call up reservists” should be understood as “we are very serious about this now, we think."
>
> In general, the president’s l[atest public appearance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EMsrcC67iTk) and strange entourage (surrounded by Amazons frozen like statues) does not add optimism. Although Putin tried to broadcast several conflicting signals to the outside world at once, reflecting the imbalance of the twilight state of his soul, the overall vector remains negative: We will bury you. This, of course, is Putin putting a brave face on an already awful situation. Nevertheless, purely theoretically, he still has a ghost of a chance to slip out of the mousetrap.
>
> Zelensky has to keep in mind two objective circumstances: the ability and readiness of Russia to launch one or more limited-yield nuclear strikes with virtually impunity. The West’s position non-intervention under no circumstances has already been voiced, i.e., NATO's refusal to resolve the issue of a “no-fly” zone over Ukrainian skies, NATO proclamation that it is not a party to the conflict. Despite the resistance of the Ukrainian army and volunteer fighters against Russian invaders, which exceeds the wildest of expectations, the continuation of the war will lead to colossal casualties for the Ukrainian people. Therefore, despite all the atrocities Russia has already committed in Ukraine, Zelensky has to think about the possibility of a compromise.
>
> But Putin's situation is not the rosiest. He has about a month left before the effect of sanctions will hit home. It will no longer be possible to fully compensate for them (until Russian citizens, for the most part, understand what, in fact, all the talk is about). The information bubble about Russia’s pinpoint strikes on military targets and small casualties will begin to burst, and prolonging the conflict will disabuse people of the notion that Russia is waging a “war of liberation.” How Russia’s population will react to repackaging his “special military operation” into a war against the “Ukes” is difficult to predict.
>
> Of course, Putin would have preferred to quickly end the operation with one technical salvo, but this has not happened. As fatigue sets in among Russian troops, such an outcome, even for Putin, should look unlikely. It seems that the Kremlin is planning another “savage act” for the sake of achieving a quick victory. To pull it off, it is necessary to use a "painful technique" - either carpet bombing with simple weapons, or precision strikes with nuclear weapons. It is not a hard decision to make. In practice, though, it is to equate oneself with Milosevic in Srebrenica. I think there’s only a quarter of a step left, but a certain psychological barrier has to be overcome - Milosevic didn’t end well. That is why Putin also is keeping an eye on what happens during talks in Belovezhskaya Pushcha [**read:** some kind of negotiated truce one settlement or armistice or understanding].
>
> The success of the negotiation process is directly proportional to the level of Putin's adequacy and inversely proportional to the level of fear of senior military and civilian officials. In any case, they are "near zero," but not yet equal to zero. If the negotiation process ends with anything, the regime can continue bargaining for a couple years. But if talks fail, then a zero-sum game begins: either a coup before the third world war erupts, or a full-blown third world war before the coup. The odds are roughly equal. Plus, it’ll happen pretty fast. I estimate it will all play out in the next six months.

## Day 14

2022-03-09 · What will happen next can't be avoided

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/be6ca227-4ad9-48b1-bcac-dc48a6840c50_658x346.jpeg)  
*Illustration c/o Clodia Barvinskaya*

Over the last two weeks, Russia’s “further military invasion” has followed the script of the latest post-apocalyptic installment of the Road Warrior franchise. The story follows the the archetypical "Western” frontier movie motif, as deepwoken legionnaires rediscover their humanity, travel to a foreign land and help local inhabitants liquidate marauders led by Putin’s chef.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a7b5ceb5-56ef-493b-af7c-0e3e63aa1477_720x581.jpeg)  
*Volunteer fighters from the United States, UK, Sweden, Lithuania, Brazil, Canada, Poland, Georgia, etc. have arrived.*

###### 

Whether Ukraine requires the additional fire power to prevent the situation from devolving further into barbarity is no longer debatable. Its own army, territorial defense units and volunteer hit squads have done an excellent job, so far. The last time I checked, officials had even [released](https://www.pravda.com.ua/rus/news/2022/02/27/7326457/) members of the Tornado death squad from prison to help keep the peace.

As of March 8, 2022, in Kyiv region the orcs are physically resident only in the part of the map shaded in red. They ride around in armored vehicles, loot, and shoot dead civilians who get in their way. Finding and vaporizing them shouldn’t be difficult. It ain’t rocket science. More like pest control.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/0b8db92e-41b8-4e89-8993-ba1f090eafab_714x885.jpeg)  
*Map c/o Kyiv State Regional Administration*

---

#### What Tiger Team Thinks

Trying to figure out what Putin is planning is no longer very interesting, because he has already “zeroed” himself by signing off on the current mess and hinting that he’s prepared to push the red button if the United States and NATO intervene on Ukraine’s behalf.

The realization of the simple fact that Putin might actually resort to nuclear bombs to subdue Ukraine has led to a result that was exactly the opposite of what Kremlin planners were counting on. That’s because what will happen cannot be avoided. If someone wants to kill himself, there’s little you can do to prevent it. An increasing number of western leaders are thinking that if armageddon is indeed inevitable, then why bother delaying it.

There are plenty, maybe too many, Ukrainian YouTube celebrities analyzing the war w/ guests every day, sometimes several times every day.

Foreign media coverage of the mess is spotty. Most mornings, [CNN](http://cnn.com) and [NYT](http://nyt.com) flaks have their ears to the ground, but their asses are on a bed in some Kyiv hotel. Don’t know what’s going on with [The Guardian](https://guardian.co.uk) or [WAPO](https://wapo.st). Same goes for [BBC.](https://bbc.co.uk)

[Kyiv Independent](https://kyivindependent.com/) still doing some stuff, but mostly from the couch. Same goes for Kyiv’s [worst blimp crash ever](https://kyivpost.com). Jornos employed by the latter were among the first to beat feet. I saw Efrem from [AP](https://ap.com) yesterday, so they’re still taking pictures. The team from [Reuters](https://reuters.com) is also lurking in the shadows.

What’s-his-face Podoliak, who advises the President’s Office, has said more than 2,000 foreign jornos are in Ukraine reporting about the mess.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/12740dbe-baed-4395-876d-728d05d65fc8_894x953.jpeg)

###### 

**Russia has lost the war**, and the map above is misleading. The same goes for dozens of forecasts and studies published by [Institute for the Study of War](https://www.criticalthreats.org/the-project) and [Critical Threats](https://www.understandingwar.org/) over the six months leading up to the invasion. Both 501© think tanks are the brainchildren of Fred, who probably means well, but doesn’t have a clue.

Looking on the positive side, had it not been for Fred’s [page](https://www.aei.org/podcast/ukraine-update-frederick-kagan-on-the-jordan-b-peterson-podcast/) at the American Enterprise Institute, I wouldn’t have reminisced about Michael Solo, a Russian emigre and friend of the family who taught military history at West Point before Fred arrived there. It was Fred’s chit chat with Jordan Peterson, titled “[Ukraine Update,](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YDElXTPm3VQ&t=2s)” that led me to take a closer look at AEI’s/ISW’s reports and maps.

Beltway bullshit about off-ramps for Russia abounds. I usually pay more attention to what Russians are saying.

Experts in the West who are trying to figure out why Fred’s assessments have been consistently flawed should put on thinking caps. Unfortunately, many do not even speak or understand Russian or Ukrainian.

In a nutshell, Putin is and remains an *irrational actor* who is *less* well-grounded in reality than most world leaders. “Bounded rationality” is *irrelevant* for explaining the Russian leader’s decisions or actions. Rinse, repeat.

And [this](https://x.com/TulsiGabbard/status/1502960938147729413) just in from Tulsi:

> “There are 25+ US-funded biolabs in Ukraine which if breached would release & spread deadly pathogens to US/world. We must take action now to prevent disaster. US/Russia/Ukraine/NATO/UN/EU must implement a ceasefire now around these labs until they’re secured & pathogens destroyed.”

We are at a disadvantage, because no one knows the end of the story. We can guess what it will be, but only partially.

What meaning should we attach to Russia’s complete collapse after Putin’s failed *special operation*? Will it turn out to be the realization of the oldest of dreams if Ukraine survives?

Or a nightmare about genocidal massacre, if it doesn't, like over Mariupol this morning?

Last time I checked, we concluded *before* Putin decided to invade, that there were only two possible outcomes of the current mess: Continuation of WW4 or a coup. Biden and European leaders and NATO have been very late waking up to this fact.

Save diplomacy for after WW4.

## Day 21

2022-03-16 · Nothing glorious about war

Text within this block will maintain its original spacing when published

```
There is nothing glorious about war.
```

Text within this block will maintain its original spacing when published

```
Whatever we write about it or think about it
```

Text within this block will maintain its original spacing when published

```
belies the unspeakable tragedy of it.
```

Rory always comes to mind when war reporters bite the dust. He and his new wife rented the house of my driver’s wife in Peredelkino outside of Moscow. They built a production studio in the back yard, rented a horse and built a small stable. It was an ideal set up. There were lots of parties, before he was [shot dead in the neck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rory_Peck) outside of Ostankino. Michael Dasaro was [carved up](https://www.theatlantic.com/magazine/archive/1994/06/the-wild-east/376360/) a couple of months later. Galling that Simpson and Hersh eulogized them, respectively.

---

Russia’s ongoing war against Ukraine, [we are told](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/03/11/opinion/russia-sanctions.html), is an attempt “to break Ukraine by demolishing its cities and brutalizing its people.”

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/44a756d1-f341-44eb-a9ad-6a11776fb4c4_698x226.png)

I thought the \*interlacing\* part went out the window in 1993. Or, at least, by 1997, when I watched this performance live in Paris lying horizontal in a bed from a loft after too much wine. Then came 1998, Belgrade, and 1999.

---

[Ed Barnes](http://content.time.com/time/world/article/0,8599,95399,00.html) filled me in later about the Yugoslavian wars in Munich. In 2011, he posted a photograph of the Time Magazine’s pickup, which wound up in the Newseum as an exhibit. It looks like a Nissan.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/363a199e-9676-4c14-90fa-535e0678e419_594x391.jpeg)

> This was the last ride for the beast. I drove it to Zagreb. It was an amazing ride. People would look at the damage and figured it was a war criminal escaping and got out of the way. But there were the rides to Pale through the snow drifts and tank rts. And it had only three tapes to listen to - Neil Young, heavy metal and a standard one. They fit every occasion. Heavy metal if you were being shot at, Neil Young for when you didn't know what you were doing and one for regular driving.

---

Still digressing.

---

So depressing.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/769104f4-071d-4689-a3a8-a4a4f8a769c9_1426x927.png)

## Day 22

Still shaken from the sacking of [Hoholiv](https://kyiv.comments.ua/ua/news/society/accidents/8304-rosiyski-okupanti-zruynuvali-selo-pid-brovarami-foto.html) due [east](https://www.google.com/maps/place/Hoholiv,+Kyiv+Oblast,+07452/@50.2447008,30.8488009,9.25z/data=!4m5!3m4!1s0x40d4dffc6583164d:0x5e25e8870fc225d0!8m2!3d50.509297!4d31.0160571) of frontline Kyiv, our battle-weary war NBC chief foreign correspondent, doing his usual shabby Clarissa Ward impersonation, [fantasizes](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M7XkafMUtuc) that Lukianivska is located in the center of the capital.

It isn’t.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/aeee2d30-a8ea-4a1b-b4dc-1d7ebe9f0cac_300x181.png)

Amid all the death and sadness, scenes from [Russia’s siege of Mariupol](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9bTEHeLCpVk) remain the most horrific, with an advisor to the city saying there could be around [20,000 civilian casualties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Mariupol). That’s about three times the number of people massacred in Srebnica.

Looking on the bright side, it’s obvious Russia will be defeated.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/99fa9a57-1569-44b6-b2dd-ffeb03fed91f_1280x851.jpeg)  
*My toes and Sam in Hoholiv after too much barbecue ( 2007 )*

---

Peanut gallery Ukraine experts still failing to grasp that what’s transpiring isn’t a war against Ukraine, per se, but another bloody episode in Putin’s years-long crusade against *liberalism*, in the broadest sense of the word.

The United States is **Advising** and **Assisting** Ukraine in its war against Russia, the [Det-A](https://detachment-a.org/detachment-a-how-special-forces-soldiers-operated-undercover-in-cold-war-berlin/) equivalent of *suggesting* the best place to cross the death strip in Cold War East Berlin. The decision on *authorizing* the third A - **Accompanying** - should be a couple weeks, maybe months, down the road.

The decision to deploy U.S. and/or NATO troops will likely depend on how many thousands more innocent Ukrainian civilians are blown to bits by errant Russian cruise missiles and indiscriminate shelling by multiple-rocket launchers. False-flag limited nuclear and chemical attacks might be triggers, but no one knows. So far, Biden and Jens have been mostly talking about what they’re not going to do.

---

Today we mark the 25th anniversary of being [expelled](https://abdymok.blogspot.com/1997/03/expulsion.html) from Belarus and the start of the Ukraine experiment via Warsaw, Budapest, Belgrade and [Millwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millwood,_New_York).

And we celebrate Balush’s birthday. All day long. He turned nine.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/3c7b99b3-8448-49e2-aa77-0b845c7c0abb_930x960.jpeg)

Happy birthday, Balush!

---

Wednesday was quiet in Kyiv during the day. For the past week or so, the Russians have been lobbing in cruise missiles, usually early in the morning, hitting residential buildings in northern districts of the city.

**Some positive developments:**

- Ukraine’s SBU State Security Service [nabbed](https://kiev.vgorode.ua/news/sobytyia/a1200787-v-kieve-zaderzhali-svjashchennika-upts-kotoryj-sotrudnichal-so-spetssluzhbami-vraha) Onufriy the UOC (MP) priest for collaborating with Russian invaders. Caught him in his underpants.
- Chechen fighters from the Sheikh Mansur Battalion began [mop-up-orc ops](https://t.me/The3rdForceUA/2654) in Kyiv suburbs.
- Truth laboratory has denied reported rumors (by [FT, of course](https://www.ft.com/content/7b341e46-d375-4817-be67-802b7fa77ef1)) of a draft peace deal reached between Ze and Putin. (cf. Pastukhov)
- Unshaven Ze addressed both houses of the U.S. Congress. I liked the last part of the [performance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gGHFvfBThUY).
- The brother of a friend (and his wife) managed to escape Mariupol by car.
- Successful tooth removal c/o Evhen.

## Day 24

2022-03-19 · More Smart Optics, Please

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a10df13c-5b61-4631-9753-96dd821fafe5_846x666.png)  
*Russia-Ukraine Monitor Map is a crowdsourced effort by CIR*

As Russia’s further invasion of Ukraine enters its fourth week, orienting yourself from Kyiv has become a little bit easier, thanks to the boys and girls at [Centre for Information Resilience](https://www.info-res.org/) (CIR) and [MapHub](https://maphub.net/).

Several forward lines of own troops (FLOTs) have been established around the western, northern and eastern sides of capital. The orcs are no longer attacking, but hiding in surrounding towns and villages, terrorizing civilians and doing everything they can to stay alive.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/12ae597c-87c6-467c-84dc-b7d9e99e3636_990x716.png)

To the West of Kyiv is an ocean of flat fields (well, a bracelet of small sparsely-populated towns, then Zhytomyr and Lviv, the large islands of Poland and Germany, then an ocean). To the North is Chernihiv and a huge smear of boggy marshes extending into Belarus and western Russia called the Polesian Lowland, or Polissia. Monotonous terrain stretches to the East, all the way to Donbas, reminding us that planet is indeed an oblate spheroid. Odesa and the Black Sea are almost due South, with Cherkasy, Kremenchuk, Dnipro and Zaporizhia straddling the Dnipro - the fourth longest river in Europe. The hilliest part - the Carpathian mountain range - is tucked in the southwestern corner of Zakarpattia bordering Romania, Slovakia and Romania.

Fred at the [Institute for the Study of War](https://www.understandingwar.org/) provides us this [map](https://twitter.com/TheStudyofWar/status/1504950017542533131/photo/3) (as of March 18) illustrating Russia’s attempt to encircle Kyiv. [Critical Threats](https://www.criticalthreats.org/analysis/russian-offensive-campaign-assessment-march-18), a U.S. taxpayer-funded slush fund, provides a full report.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/decf3e08-1146-49a2-8e3e-a31354677c12_1287x1229.jpeg)

Compare it with Kyiv Region State Military Administration’s map (also as of March 18) [here](https://t.me/kievreal1/13883).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a52896c1-f9a0-437a-9fdd-26e30133ab1c_892x1099.jpeg)

Tik Tok influencers, meanwhile, have identified at least three individuals directly responsible for the unspeakable tragedy.

Repeated statements by western leaders, including Joe and Olaf, about not being parties to the war are curious. ![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/b4f5c32f-9071-44c2-a6e2-28b20a45292f_1240x684.jpeg)

---

Spandau Prison used to be clearly visible from [Teufelsberg](https://abdymok.medium.com/the-hill-fce76b85bb86) in Forst Grunewald in western Berlin before it was turned into a shopping mall. The castle was home to seven top Nazi leaders convicted in the Nuremberg trials, including [Alfred Speer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Speer#Imprisonment), who spent 20 years there. Speer later became a best selling author - *[Inside the Third Reich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inside_the_Third_Reich)* (in German, *Erinnerungen*, or *Reminiscences*) and *[Spandau: The Secret Diaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spandau:_The_Secret_Diaries)* (in German, *Spandauer Tagebücher*).

Joe and Olaf should keep this passage in mind:

> *“Despite the ideological kinship and growing personal ties, the decision was not an easy one. For several more days, Hitler grimly remarked that the situation had forced him to take this step, and was greatly relieved when the sanctions adopted in the final vote a few weeks later turned out to be relatively mild. From what happened, Hitler concluded: both England and France do not want to take risks and seek to avoid the slightest danger. His actions, which later seemed reckless, were the result of precisely those observations. Western governments, as he commented at the time, showed their weakness and indecision. His opinion was confirmed on March 7, 1936, when German troops entered the demilitarized Rhine zone, which was an open violation of the Locarno Treaty and could provoke military countermeasures from the Allied countries. In nervous excitement, Hitler waited for the first reaction. In all the compartments of the special train on which we were traveling to Munich, a tense atmosphere reigned, the epicenter of which was the compartment of the Fuhrer. At one of the stations, a message was brought to Hitler, and he breathed a sigh of relief: ‘Finally! The English king will not interfere. He keeps his promise. This means that everything will be fine…’ In any case, even after the message he did not calm down, and even later, when he was already at war against almost the whole world, he always called the entry of troops into the Rhine zone his most daring action.”*

Haven’t read Stefan’s [sitrep](https://www.facebook.com/stefan.korshak) yet or listened to Mark’s latest [vlog](https://www.youtube.com/channel/UChLpUGaZO35ICTltBP50VSg/videos). Lots of great photographs and dire-sounding headlines at the Guardian, CNN, WAPO, etc. I liked Carlotta’s [piece](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/03/20/world/europe/ukraine-war-kyiv.html?searchResultPosition=2) & photos about Kyiv in the NYT.

Andriy on [Telegram](https://t.me/Tsaplienko) says enemy agent groups are running amok in Kyiv, that defense units rolled up 149 saboteurs yesterday. One of the great aspects about living under martial law is that your’e allowed to make a citizen’s arrest. Disturbing scenes of gratuitous violence in Kherson, c/o [Dvizh](https://t.me/dvish_alive). I took a boo at Z’s monologues, [one](https://t.me/V_Zelenskiy_official) of which - the chit chat w/ foreign jornos - is almost an hour long.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a7cf09a7-b7d9-4deb-83d9-d84fda27f9b2_419x648.png)

Or maybe it’s a different American TikTok legionnaire wandering around the outskirts of Kyiv. There are lots of them. Sheikh Mansur Battalion fighters are the real deal.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a92d37f1-ba4b-4bdc-875f-474a494322a2_892x1088.png)

---

Almost no one understands - and certainly not Mad Max - the scale of what is happening.

Instinctively, politicians in the United States and Europe are trying to figure out how Russia’s further invasion is likely to pan out. They think **mistakenly** that there will be time to adapt, course correct and eventually grow accustomed whatever the result is. In fact, we are smack dab in the middle of a catastrophe of such magnitude that there is no long-term development scenario. Not one. Anyone who thinks there is plenty of time to think the mess through should have their head examined.

We are at stage five of a four-stage disaster management response.

---

Verkhovna Rada Commissioner for Human Rights Liudmyla Denisova on March 24, 2022 [accused](https://www.facebook.com/denisovaombudsman/videos/971688386885235/) Russia of forcibly displacing, that is deporting, **hundreds of thousands** of Ukainians and funneling them to “filtration camps” in Russia before re-settling them east of the Urals. Sound familiar? It should.

Even worse, foreign media are reiterating Moscow’s justification for committing this war crime without pointing out to their readers that what the Kremlin says is complete bullshit.

c/o [Nebi and Cara](https://apnews.com/article/russia-ukraine-kyiv-europe-nato-5af548b472ba5a3ea4c64d4ef0aa4234) at Associated Press:

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a7cb0c64-84b9-485f-995f-7660d39f97a2_550x329.png)

Since at least 2015, I have unsuccessfully argued that using terms like **rebels,** **separatists**, **breakaway regions** and **insurrection** to describe Russia’s invasion of Ukraine serves **no purpose of diplomacy or journalistic balance**. It is a **failure** to serve the public interest. Russia’s war against Ukraine should be described as what it is: **an armed conflict instigated and sustained by Russia’s armed intervention**.

To imply that there has been any acts of **self-determination** by shell-shocked refugees from Mariupol, Chernihiv, Kharkiv, Sumy, Berdiansk or from any besieged city of Ukraine to seek refuge in Russia contradicts the highest available organized expressions of international law. If editors, journalists, politicians and pundits decide to regurgitate Kremlin talking points, then, at least, they should explain why.

So, don’t be like Nebi and Cara and their editors.

---

In other news, Timothy Snyder wrote an [op-ed](https://www.washingtonpost.com/opinions/2022/03/23/putin-genocide-language-ukraine-wipe-out-state-identity/) for the Washington Post worth reading.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/0de6a1ae-750d-4837-9425-5fa28ff46329_547x255.png)

And London-based practicing philosopher Vladimir Pastukhov yesterday shared his thoughts about the catastrophe on [YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lynO_pN7frU). Leonid, meanwhile, is taking a breather from the mess, but [promised](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=R7q_Uu3ohNs&t=11s) to talk about it again in a couple of weeks.

---

Last item: Andy. Kramer’s [beat](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/03/24/world/europe/ukraines-troops-begin-counteroffensive-that-alters-shape-of-the-battle-with-russia.html?searchResultPosition=1) these daze is the counteroffensive around Kyiv: wanna-be Det-A, DEVGRU guys wandering around the capital’s northwestern suburbs blowing stuff up, making 30-second videos and posting them to the Internet.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/77d77527-35d5-4202-89bd-2b9d1fa1206d_583x211.png)

My advice is to ignore Andy. Time better spent digesting Violetta’s [Battle for Moshchun](https://censor.net/ru/resonance/3328167/bitva_za_moschun_forpost_kiva) (Krynytsia) reportage. This is the very same village where I lost my silver-framed custom-made NYC eyeglasses in a deep swimming hole during the very hot summer of 1991. I woke up the next morning under a tree in Kyiv.

There's something about senseless destruction in this photograph, which evokes the *Eternal Struggle*, the 1923 American silent drama film.

Outdoor living and foraging for food can be fun for a couple of days if it’s for recreation, but it’s a miserable lifestyle.

## Day 4, Phase 2

2022-03-29 · S/Lab Ultra 3

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/c405564b-82a7-424b-bad6-62a326d8cc9b_3311x2394.jpeg)  
*Nathan’s map .*

###### 

\*\*\*

*London-based practicing philosopher Vladimir Pastukhov [answers](https://t.me/v_pastukhov/84) the question, “When will it end?”*

No one knows, and no one knows what “it” is.

**Cut-offs:**

- Two months, approximately until June (it will become clear whether it is possible to conquer Ukraine, without using nuclear weapons)
- Four-five months, approximately until August (it will become clear whether it is possible to conquer Ukraine in principle)
- 12-14 months, approximately until the summer of next year (it will become clear whether Russia can survive in isolation without oil and gas trade with Europe)

Each next cut-off can only be reached by surviving the previous one. After each cut-off, some new reality, or unreality will be established.

Pastukhov’s extended remarks for Current Time are [here](https://youtube.com/watch?v=60Kssz48GAs&feature=share).

---

[The Washington Post](https://www.washingtonpost.com/world/2022/03/28/ukraine-russia-pow-video/?itid=hp-top-table-main) and [The New York Times](https://www.nytimes.com/live/2022/03/28/world/ukraine-russia-war/a-video-shows-russian-prisoners-of-war-in-ukraine-being-beaten-and-shot-in-their-legs) latch on to war-adjacent content milling, specifically the POW kneecapping video posted by TikTok-ers the day before [yesterday](https://www.nytimes.com/live/2022/03/28/world/ukraine-russia-war/a-video-shows-russian-prisoners-of-war-in-ukraine-being-beaten-and-shot-in-their-legs). ![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/039d58bc-9295-4a31-98e4-30a7fc010342_1016x601.png)

As we attempted to point out diplomatically on [Day 18](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/day-18?s=w) (Fred's Non-predictive Intelligence Floppery), Putin remains an irrational actor who is less well-grounded in reality than most world leaders. “[Bounded rationality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bounded_rationality)” is irrelevant for explaining the Russian leader’s decisions or actions.

This might explain my skepticism about the prospects for a ceasefire in the foreseeable future. The chatter in Istanbul yesterday reminded me of Tarkovsky’s final movie, [The Sacrifice](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=psBqOWzHwS4), specifically the bewildered reaction of the witch after the protagonist puts a gun to his temple.

> “Don’t kill us.”

The jet fighters return… They make love…. And float above the bed.

So the story goes.

Ibrahim and Leonid produced this excellent [breakdown](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=txo1A3_QeFg) of the “Turkey Talks.”

---

Some 17,300 Russian soldiers have not died in vain during the further invasion of Ukraine, according to the proposed terms for ending the bloody mess.

As a result of Putin’s special operation, Russia and Ukraine:

- **agree** to talk about the status of Crimea for the next 15 years. Before the invasion, Russia said there was nothing to discuss.
- **agree** that NATO weapons, which were not in Ukraine, will not be in Ukraine.
- **agree** that Ukraine will accelerate EU integration and receive financial assistance.
- **agree** that Ukraine will receive NATO-like security guarantees from United States, Germany, France, Israel, Turkey, et cetera, without paying.

## Day 6, Phase 2

2022-03-31 · From Zero to Hero

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/7f21dbac-781d-44ca-94d1-75ad6d8db8f9_1280x960.jpeg)  
*Photo c/o Bochkala\_# Somehwere in Ukraine*

We know knowledge matters, but although we know that Americans who are avid TikTok users, for example, behave differently than those don’t, it is less clear whether this difference stems from Gen Z putting its command of facts to good use, or to certain types of people with unhealthy news-consumption habits.

The answer might explain why most Americans (72%) have a lot or some confidence in Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky, higher than any other international leader asked about in a new Pew Research Center [survey](https://www.pewresearch.org/fact-tank/2022/03/30/zelenskyy-inspires-widespread-confidence-from-u-s-public-as-views-of-putin-hit-new-low/).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/774914a3-ad5a-435c-a087-6bafe63d5bc7_427x380.png)

> Almost half of Americans ages 65 and older have *a* *lot* of confidence in Zelensky, compared with 28% of adults under 30. Americans with more education, especially those with postgraduate degrees, are also more inclined to have confidence in the Ukrainian president, the report [says](https://twitter.com/pewresearch).

The vast majority of Americans with poor geographic and foreign policy knowledge are the most supportive of Zelensky. At the same time, less than 7% of those surveyed were able to find his country on a map. So it goes.

---

---

It’s churlish to dwell on all that went wrong with professional journalism in Ukraine. True, some Kyiv-based journalists aren’t completely annoying to be in the same room with, and you have to admire the tenacity to remain in a profession wherein rejection and slamming criticism are just the beginning.

Gaping insecurity, white-hot neediness, gelatin bravado – there are worse characteristics to have, but these traits can be overlooked during a war if you’re 1) in love or 2) in power.

---

Which brings me to The Washington Post’s [editorial page](https://www.washingtonpost.com/opinions/?itid=hp_top_nav_opinions), which is bending over backwards for Ze!Team.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/281b91dd-79bf-47b0-9317-f789a7cca1cb_488x221.png)

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/ddc3cc45-f836-46ac-ace5-bd4a75c0d42e_786x941.jpeg)

The real appeal is the headline: after the original laugh, it might as well read *Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet....* Next stop, [Channel 7](https://7kanal.com.ua/), [Oprah](https://www.oprah.com/index.html) and The Washington Post’s [TikTok page](https://www.tiktok.com/@washingtonpost).

And then there’s Andriy Yermak, the unexpected and un-elected head of the President’s Office, who [believes](https://www.washingtonpost.com/opinions/2022/03/21/ukraine-zelenskyy-the-west-underestimated-our-bravery-russia-brutality-civilians/) Ze is the “undisputed leader” of the “new world order.”

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/8c388795-1faf-49b5-8e80-56536f92d270_702x273.png)

## Day 7, Phase 2

2022-04-01 · Dyversanti? Diversionists?

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/8c749635-f42e-49a2-bf7f-2b76bbb1eaf0_1280x852.jpeg)  
*Photo by Mssrs: Aris Messinis, Alex Kent, Chris McGrath, Sergi Mykhalchuk*

Ze’s official [Telegram](https://t.me/V_Zelenskiy_official) posts photographs taken in all parts of Ukraine [daily](https://t.me/V_Zelenskiy_official/1037?single). So does the [Strategic Communications Department of the Armed Forces of Ukraine.](https://www.facebook.com/AFUStratCom)

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/9e475e52-a6a1-47d5-a4af-b0300b18b4e2_1280x948.jpeg)  
*Sunk somehwere near Sumy ( AFU Stratcom )*

There is little reason to be optimistic that Russia’s invasion of Ukraine will end any time in the near future. That means lots more unspeakable tragedy and death. Looking on the positive side, warm damp weather has arrived and the scenery will change over the coming days and weeks.

Lots of linage about Ukraine in the international press, much of it coming from Lviv. The first article that catches my eye is “[A Nation of Spy-Catchers: Fear of Saboteurs Has Ukrainians on Edge](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/03/31/world/europe/ukraine-spies-saboteurs.html)” by Valerie Hopkins of The New York Times.

I stopped reading after paragraph number six:

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/7cdf2520-de5e-4f4d-b681-e325151f58b9_589x165.png)

*Dyversanti? Diversionists?*

---

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/fa49984f-5bb5-44b0-84b4-a4dd0daa8659_1090x604.png)

---

---

Next up: “[Russian soldiers in Ukraine sabotaged own equipment, U.K. spy chief says](https://www.washingtonpost.com/world/2022/03/31/russian-soldiers-morale-sabotage-equipment-war/?itid=hp-top-table-main)” by Meryl Kornfield and Amy Cheng, appearing in The Washington Post.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/3106b38d-f6eb-4438-8740-9c07ae3977ed_538x233.png)

Indeed, altho it’s better much better than merely “extremely effective.” It’s fucking “[brilliant](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/lost-in-translation?s=w),” according to Mykhailo Podoliak, an advisor to Andriy Yermak, head of the President’s Office, who on March 21, 2022 [announced](https://www.google.com/url?sa=t&rct=j&q=&esrc=s&source=web&cd=&cad=rja&uact=8&ved=2ahUKEwjissiz7PH2AhWEgv0HHYm6CugQFnoECBUQAQ&url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.washingtonpost.com%2Fopinions%2F2022%2F03%2F21%2Fukraine-zelenskyy-the-west-underestimated-our-bravery-russia-brutality-civilians%2F&usg=AOvVaw2_lWlaI24qmBDCyx2RM-Ck) in Meryl’s and Amy’s newspaper that Ze is today **[The Leader of The New World Order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_world_order_(politics))**. Yermak [doubled down](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5PSM7L33Zzg) on the wacko assertion days later in broken English during an interview with CNN’s Christian Amanpour.

But I digress.

I immediately disregarded what Britain’s spy chief said in the article, but did pay attention to commentary by [Alexey Muraiev](https://staffportal.curtin.edu.au/staff/profile/view/alexey-muraviev-8770eee9/), a national security expert at Australia’s Curtin University.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/f27f9050-be9f-4c5f-9412-516929bddd06_562x180.jpeg)

No one has yet come up with a suitable punishment for Ukraine experts who, in the ninth year of the war, deploy the **Russian-backed separatist troops** linguistic *trope.*

---

Nathan from Lviv for CNN also mentions Britain’s spy chief whoever and provides *[analysis](https://edition.cnn.com/2022/03/31/europe/ukraine-russia-troops-regrouping-intl-cmd/index.html)* using correctly transliterated public-facing place names, except the already [noted](https://edition.cnn.com/2022/03/31/europe/ukraine-russia-troops-regrouping-intl-cmd/index.html) and complained-about Zaporizh*zh*ia exception.

---

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/7a9b80e8-f916-46d9-b9d4-be12161cea1c_1068x623.jpeg)  
*Rhys Byrne ready to defend Ukraine, c/o Extra.ie*

And finally there is Rhys, maybe a distant cousin, who is now on the record saying he will “probably die” during his Ukraine deployment.

*Dear Rhys, don’t be so fatalistic. Next time you’re in Kyiv, drop me a line and we can hook, go for a fast run together, roll around the mats, shoot guns and perfect our knife throwing skills.*

2022-04-03 · Jingoism-Resistant Rebel Bullshit

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/806cc37d-c597-44af-971d-44ed193236d4_1280x853.jpeg)

It’s a quiet cold Sunday morning in Kyiv. I sit down to read about Ukraine…

Five minutes later, I’m stuck in the middle of a 6,800-word Wall Street Journal [recapitulation](https://www.wsj.com/articles/vladimir-putins-20-year-march-to-war-in-ukraineand-how-the-west-mishandled-it-11648826461) of the entire mess, titled “Vladimir Putin’s 20-Year March to War in Ukraine—and How the West Mishandled It.”

Six co-authors of the feature say CIA Director William Burns visited Kyiv in early January to warn Ze!Team about Putin’s invasion plans.

[We recall](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/stay-out-of-doorways?s=w) that U.S. and allied non-permissive environment specialists at the time had just landed in Kyiv and were scheming with indigenous commandos at [Tootsie Club](https://en.tootsie.kiev.ua/) to liberate possible future oppressed residents of the capital.

## Day 10, Phase 2

2022-04-04 · One Big Crime Scene

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/4b39be75-8cdf-48c2-9a2c-a44b102f3d6e_1230x627.jpeg)  
*Photo c/o Стопкор*

The “[vile purulence](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/a-separate-reality?s=w)“ London-based practicing philosopher Vladimir Pastukhov told us about ***before the invasion started*** was on full display over the weekend for foreign photographers wandering around Kyiv’s northwestern suburbs (Iprin, Bucha, [Borodyanka](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/day-9?s=w), Vorzel, Moshchun).

It’s too bad the world wasn’t paying attention when we experienced a similar nightmare nine years ago in Donbas. At the time, jingoism-resistant expats filed reports about pro-Russian separatist groups revolting against the government in Kyiv.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/8d098b4d-a629-417c-a065-31dd35865d9d_379x416.png)

This time around, there are plenty of fingerprints, thanks to Tiger Team, drones, very public secret state intelligence, including SIGINT.

Pictures of the most recent carnage show us what Putin’s Special Operation in Ukraine is really all about. Humanitarian and legal aspects of the botched campaign will be investigated by international commissions for years.

Any army that robs and rapes civilians is not an effective fighting force. The collection of out-of-work mercenaries and untrained peasants sent to pillage towns around Kyiv is not a regular, combat-ready force, just a bunch of undisciplined licensed privateers, much like in Donbas nine years ago.

The three-hour plus video below, recorded from 11:30 to 15:00 on Saturday, April 2, 2022, documents them sending home loot at [Russia-registered CDEK](https://www.cdek.ru/ru/offices/view/moz2-na-kuybysheva-32-ul-kuybysheva-32) express delivery service on [Kuibysheva street, 32](https://www.google.com/maps/place/Ulitsa+Kuybysheva+32,+Mazyr,+Belarus/@52.0510268,29.2503971,18z/data=!4m5!3m4!1s0x46d6122defb8fcfd:0x3cf735073b8eec28!8m2!3d52.0508899!4d29.2514002) in Mazyr, Belarus.

My attention turns to Russia’s Truth Ministry and the *[manifest](https://censor.net/ru/resonance/3331448/vd_tez_pro_vi_runa_do_zaklikv_do_genotsidu_ukrantsv_chomu_stattyu_timofya_sergeyitseva_vipustili_pslya)* appearing on April 4, 2022 in its flagship RIA Novosti, titled [What Russia Should Do With Ukraine](https://ria.ru/20220403/ukraina-1781469605.html).

> “The Nazis who took up arms should be destroyed to the maximum on the battlefield. There should be no significant differences between the Armed Forces of Ukraine and the so-called national battalions, as well as the territorial defense that joined these two types of military formations. All of them are equally involved in extreme cruelty against the civilian population, equally guilty of the genocide of the Russian people and do not comply with the laws and customs of war. **War criminals and active Nazis should be exemplarily and demonstratively punished**. There must be a total lustration. Any organizations that have associated themselves with the practice of Nazism have been liquidated and banned. However, in addition to the top leaders, a significant part of the masses, composed of passive Nazis, are accomplices of Nazism and also guilty. They supported and indulged Nazi power. The just punishment of this part of the population is possible only as bearing the inevitable hardships of a just war against the Nazi system.”

But obvi the question we should be asking is “What should we do with Russia?”

## Day 12, Phase 2

2022-04-06 · On Becoming Ukrainian

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a6bb9a1f-8228-44b3-97c3-81642ef41eb5_1280x853.jpeg)  
*Bombed out Borodyanka photo c/o Andriy Tsaplienko*

### In Ukraine your internationalism can and should be your patriotism.

We wrote abut Borodyanka on **[Day 9](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/day-9?s=w)** of Russia’s further invasion. That was March 4. Ukraine’s top cop Iryna Venediktova [woke up](https://focus.ua/uk/ukraine/511334-v-borodyanke-naihudshaya-po-kievskoy-oblasti-situaciya-v-plane-chelovecheskih-zhertv-venediktova) to the disaster the day before yesterday, saying mass civilian killings there make the [blood bath in Bucha](https://edition.cnn.com/videos/world/2022/04/05/volodymyr-zelensky-full-united-nations-speech-bucha-massacre-sot-vpx.cnn) look like a picnic.

Let's say that more than a month *after* a mass murder had devastated Borodyanka, and at a moment when the miasma from the site could still be felt and smelled, a ticket-buying audience finally began paying attention.

For more than eight years, the world needed to know **fascists**[1](#footnote-1) in Russia were sending soldiers to Ukraine to commit **genocide**[2](#footnote-2). During most of this period, we were told told fighting in eastern Ukraine was partly a **civil war** involving **rebels** and **separatists** opposing the central government in Kyiv. The pace of senseless killing intensified in February 2022.

Political leaders in Europe and journalists talked about the bloodshed as if it was entirely internal to Ukraine, a domestic affair brought on by **language politics**, **identity clashes** and **historical grievances**. Many of the same jingoism-resistant **nincompoops** made a beeline for chocolate shops in Lviv after Putin’s *special operation* began.

**Don’t take my word for it.**

## Day 13, Phase 2

2022-04-07

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/dcd3a787-d1f7-439c-a0e8-82a86d6817dd_4096x2752.jpeg)  
*Map c/o @war\_mapper*

I’ll concentrate on the military and military-political situation today. I see Stefan has written a very, very long [brief](https://www.facebook.com/stefan.korshak/posts/5500684436622342). I congratulate him for spelling public-facing proper nouns correctly :-)

Yuriy is back from the front lines w/ this report and lots of maps:

Fred & Co continue [plotting](https://www.criticalthreats.org/) (at US taxpayer expense):

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a658d27b-fd0e-4844-aa14-5bd973b727c0_650x696.jpeg)

[Rob Picheta](https://twitter.com/robpicheta), the digital producer at CNN, appears confused about place name spellings.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/5b88bb33-0c8c-4cf1-a798-8797e4b30db4_745x134.png)

Someone, please, tell Rob that Stefan and Fred, with the exception of Zaporizh*zh*ia, transliterate place names correctly. CNN, on the other hand, maybe thanks to Rob, does not, e.g. Izyum, Slovyansk, which should be rendered Izium, Sloviansk, just like #Kyiv (not #Kiev). The Kyiv-based [Interfax-Ukraine](https://en.interfax.com.ua/) news agency remains a standard bearer for correct English-language proper noun transliteration from Ukrainian. If Rob and CNN want to transliterate place names from Russian, they should explain why.

Arkady straightens Mark out in this [analysis](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_2RQmm3_2Q) of the military-political situation.

Arkady asks an important question in the beginning of the chit chat:

**Why didn’t Ze & Co, at least, warn, if not evacuate, residents of towns north of the capital and in Kyiv/Cherkasy regions after CIA Director Robert Burns warned Ukraine’s top brass in person on January 12, 2022 that the Russians would launch a full-blown invasion, including from Belarus?**

On March 6, Mykhailo Podoliak, advisor to Andriy Yermak, head of Ukraine’s Office of the President, admitted this was a mistake, starting at 46:20, in this [chit chat](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J5dn_dPXuIY).

> Latynina: So, you’re saying that you [Ze!Team] made a mistake.
>
> Podoliak: **Yes**, to a certain extent. **Yes**.

Podoliak explains that Ze!Team made the same mistake as Fred in ISW’s loopy [counterfactual fantasy](https://www.understandingwar.org/sites/default/files/Ukraine%20Invasion%20Forecast%20Series%20Part%201%20ISW%20CT%20December%202021.pdf?fbclid=IwAR2XMXeaIPep1VRBXDubW-c3IKBPGNV9mmfL-hTJvVQ8wdEZVgDiItLb2hE) (which we discussed on [Day 5](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/day-5?s=w)), namely assessing that:

> Putin is and remains a rational actor who is not less well-grounded in reality than most world leaders. ...He will not intentionally take actions likely to risk the survival of the Russian state or his own rule, although... No “bounded rationality” is necessary to explain Putin’s decisions or actions.

Viktor Arestovych, who advises Podoliak, has a different explanation:

Arestovych says the decision not to warn Ukrainians in the path of an imminent Russian invasion was made in order to prevent people from panicking and fleeing en masse, clogging train stations, bridges, et cetera.

So, there you have it. Two not exactly mutually exclusive explanations.

(*to be continued*)

## Day 14, Phase 2

2022-04-08 · Likeable Moments of Self-deprecation

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/0ca8dc71-d3f0-4923-bc4f-fff105662298_583x248.png)  
*As Ukraine braces for a second round, the West has a duty to step up by Danid Ignatius (The Washington Post. April 8, 2002)*

\*\*\*

Maybe we should start by identifying the U.S. Defense Department officials who came to this erroneous conclusion. And put them in sacks. While we’re at it, let’s cut off funding to [Fred](https://www.criticalthreats.org/analysis/the-problem-with-a-neutral-ukraine-putin-is-as-bad-as-his-word) at the American Enterprise Institute’s [Critical Threats](https://www.criticalthreats.org/) dream factory and use the saved money to support projects managed by individuals who understand what the fuck is going on.[1](#footnote-1)

#### Some simple guidelines:

- Diplomacy doesn’t work with fascists intent on committing a genocide.[2](#footnote-2)
- Take what Ukrainian officials say with a grain of salt.[3](#footnote-3)
- Pick macroeconomists specializing in Ukraine carefully.[4](#footnote-4)
- It’s difficult for someone to accept outside surgical assessments when she’s on the operating table.[5](#footnote-5)

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/f6464122-5b44-4247-9446-c17050c4edae_1080x720.jpeg)  
*Photo c/o Третя Сила*

Needless to say, I remain disappointed with reports about the involvement of Belarus in Russia’s further invasion of Ukraine.

A close reading of the increasingly voluminous revisionist *reports* discloses many further examples of events that one thinks cannot really be true, or cannot be true if the quasi-official or consecrated narrative is to remain regnant.

- Has [Brian Whitmore](https://www.atlanticcouncil.org/expert/brian-whitmore/) ever actually been in Minsk?
- How can anyone take [Grigoriy Ioffe](https://jamestown.org/program/divining-belaruss-future-amidst-international-and-domestic-unrest/) seriously?
- When did [Ilya Ponomarev](https://www.wsj.com/articles/belarusians-russians-join-ukraines-military-hoping-for-freedom-at-home-11649325276) become an expert on Belarus?

Let’s beef up our cadre of Belarus experts, instead of reinforcing a series of tropes that were already familiar to students of ready-made gepolitical rhetoric.

Before it’s too late.

## Day 15, Phase 2

2022-04-09 · Unaccountably Mislaid Solopsism

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/081cbe77-1815-4f50-bce1-eca32f9f0ca2_3311x2394.jpeg)

Over the last month and a half, we have alternatively explained, described and compared the prelude and execution of the ongoing unspeakable tragedy in various ways - from an episode of the post-apocalyptic Mad Max action film series[1](#footnote-1) to an improvisation of novel inspired by [Between the Buried and Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Between_the_Buried_and_Me)[2](#footnote-2), the final act of the ballet Gayane[3](#footnote-3), a dialogue from Motl, Peysi the Cantor's Son[4](#footnote-4) and the witch scene from a WWIII capstone film[5](#footnote-5).

A couple of days ago, we arrived at a blunt two-word assessment, replete w/ footnoted definitions, because the meaning the words *trigger* should be clearly understood, especially since your life or your death may depend on it[6](#footnote-6).

Which brings me Barrack Obama, who has also been thinking and talking about our bloody mess and its origins, [here,](https://www.theatlantic.com/ideas/archive/2022/04/barack-obama-interview-disinformation-ukraine/629496/) at least, for more than one hour.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/f4ded56f-ff7c-4f3d-b2eb-d394ba7d6889_738x665.png)

But we’ll save that for later.

## Day 17, Phase 2

2022-04-11 · Rape Joke Gone Wrong

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/4d3cde07-9aac-4f6e-9b2c-1d71ab45fe8e_1073x1017.jpeg)  
*Painting c/o Konstiantyn Kochanovsky*

Time flies. **Only two months** have passed since Putin’s beano with Macron in Moscow when he [blurted out](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/the-doorman-with-nukes-and-no-friends?s=w) that Ukraine should *bite the pillow* and fulfill *her* *duty*[1](#footnote-1).

### **Нравится, не нравится — терпи, моя красавица! Надо исполнять! По-другому не получится.**

Weeks later, on February 24, 2022, Russian troops invaded Ukraine from the north, south and east, leading to tens of thousands of gruesome deaths. The killing has not stopped since then.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/6ab0d042-d118-47cb-bc1e-f795e52111c9_4096x2752.jpeg)  
*Map c/o @ War\_mapper*

As of April 11, the General Staff of the Armed Forces of Ukraine [says](https://www.facebook.com/GeneralStaff.ua/posts/292388713074214) 19,500 Russian soldiers have been killed in action, many vaporized in short-range combat by shoulder-held high explosive anti-tank rockets, e.g. Javelin, NLAW.

When this type of projectile hits armor an explosion inside the round causes a metallic cone inside to turn into a liquid (this is called the [Munroe Effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaped_charge#Munroe_effect)) which is propelled forward in a jet and it’s this which cuts a hole through the armor. This hole isn’t very big and wouldn’t normally disable the target on its own.

What vaporizes the crew within is the “[spalling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spall)” which sees liquid metal projected into the crew compartment along with particles of armor. This spray acts like shrapnel and turns anything it comes into contact with, including the crew, into fine mulch.

([The Doorman with Nukes and No Friends](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/the-doorman-with-nukes-and-no-friends?s=w). February 9, 2022)

## Day 18, Phase 2

2022-04-12 · Divine rule and Sarin

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/5d5e6157-aded-4b29-a95d-28763609c871_1086x397.jpeg)  
*Google Ngram Viewer*

I spent a lot of time during 2014 and 2015 watching battle clips from eastern Ukraine. Most of them were uploaded to a server, downloaded, dissected, translated, titled, worded over, packed into 90-second YouTube videos, used for presentation overlays, Instagram and Facebook posts and archived for later use in documentary films.

We all remember the first and second [battles for Donetsk Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Battle_of_Donetsk_Airport) and the cyborgs who defended it. Sergei Loiko even wrote a book about it, [Airport](https://www.amazon.com/Airport-Ukrainian-Sergei-Loiko/dp/1522877878?asin=1522877878&revisionId=&format=4&depth=1), which sells for $20 on Amazon.

Today’s siege of Mariupol reminds me of the cyborgs and the months of bloody battles for Donetsk airport, including the steeplechase of hacks making hay out of unspeakable tragedy[1](#footnote-1).

**Exhibit A.** [The Kyiv Independent.](https://kyivindependent.com/)

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/fc492b2a-4dc9-4c8b-986c-66ce52d13ed7_1047x568.png)

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/92e84527-8bc9-4342-b3ae-0808253254a7_768x226.png)

The world is a lot more interested in whether Russia *actually used* chemical weapons than what confused anonymous sources representing the Office of the President, Azov regiment and The Kyiv Independent are *messaging*. The same goes for lobbing Kh-31P missiles at Kyiv[2](#footnote-2).

This might explain why Arestovych and Podoliak (and maybe now Ponomarenko) are unreliable sources of information about anything.

**Unverified reports** about the use of chemical agents made by anonymous representatives of Azov regiment are, er, clickable but not factual, [according to the Pentagon](https://www.washingtonpost.com/world/2022/04/11/russia-ukraine-war-news-putin-live-updates/#link-XETLQ6FQRZEZBHJYZHLBUGPQSY). ## Day 19, Phase 2

2022-04-13 · The immodest end to us all

Recall Phase 1 of the mess (February 24 - March 30). It ended with kitted up TikTokering legionnaires wandering around the outskirts of Kyiv taking selfies[2](#footnote-2).

We are now in the middle of Phase 2: three dozen or so Russian battalion tactical groups are getting ready to occupy all of Donbas, everything between Kharkiv in the north to Mariupol in the south.

## Day 20, Phase 2

2022-04-14

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/67a6066c-c0bc-43c8-85f8-f0ad98384b4f_1080x810.jpeg)  
*Photo c/o Bochkala\_#The War!*

We’re all familiar with the loop-the-loop problem. The basic idea is to take something and propel it around a vertical loop. [Pyotr Nesterov](https://kpi.ua/en/nesterov) dreamed up the stunt more than a century ago.

An accomplished aviator, he completed the manoeuvre on September 9, 1913 in the airspace above the Syretski military airfield in Kyiv. It was the beginning of aerobatics.

In an article, titled "How I made a loop," published in the St. Petersburg newspaper on September 5, 1913, Nesterov wrote:

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/1295ca2b-c34b-4c49-8b9c-973ab4654e06_480x204.jpeg)

Nesterov’s loop should not be confused with the [graveyard spiral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graveyard_spiral), the type of dangerous downward circling dive entered into accidentally by a pilot who is not trained or not proficient. Other names for this phenomenon include **suicide spiral**, **deadly spiral**, **death spiral** and **vicious spiral.** It’s the aviation equivalent of Putin’s decision to further invade Ukraine on February 24, 2022.

Which brings me to the mummification scenario[1](#footnote-1) proposed in an article appearing in Poligon about the Ukraine mess, titled [The Putin Crucible](https://medium.com/@abdymok/the-putin-crucible-a7b1d56f98dc).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/480850c0-5e80-4f04-ac09-ea8f3347f1eb_609x400.jpeg)

---

In other news, SIGINT, together with drone and satellite reconnaissance, has consistently produced the most reliable intelligence available since the invasion started. It’s true value as an intelligence source has been achieved because it’s been effectively combined with information produced by other intelligence sources, e.g. HUMINT, into an 'all-source' product flagged, tagged, stripped, df-ed, AI reconfigured, sometimes gisted, analyzed and funneled directly into the brains of Ukraine’s top brass.

Almost 200 reels are available for your listening pleasure [here](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ls8NOnLoLTQ&list=PL8ao2ZjeM9mslBOATvDa87lvgELw281lF).

[1](#footnote-anchor-1)

Dodging water jets is probably a reference the ingenious water features found at [Peterhof Palace.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peterhof_Palace) They are called fountain firecrackers, hidden fountains with a comic surprise, disguised as familiar objects or built into the landscape. The water jets of the fountains appear unexpectedly and spray visitors, thereby creating a playful mood. I had enormous fun dodging water jets there as a kid, especially the one disguised as a mushroom.
