## Shaping Ukraine's Strategic Objectives

2022-11-04 · Sovereignty vs. subjectiveness

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/575eedb7-d0e4-4d1f-806c-48f0f73a1f6e_1970x1693.jpeg)  
*Illustration by Nugzar Metreveli ...*

I took a week off from scowling and screaming at Tiger Team’s AGI system that refuses to be born in order to luxuriate in code that has to work on only one server, and has to be legible only to me. The bliss, I tell you, the bliss!

About that 3,000-word [whatever](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/11/02/magazine/russiagate-paul-manafort-ukraine-war.html?searchResultPosition=1) appearing in The New York Times Magazine, titled “**The Untold Story of ‘Russiagate’ and the Road to War in Ukraine.** *Russia’s meddling in Trump-era politics was more directly connected to the current war than previously understood.”*

Jim’s story is conspicuous for what he deliberately ignores, namely the preinaugural dump of [280,000 messages](https://abdymok.medium.com/killing-the-story-64bd0f6840e8) sent and received by Paul Manafort’s daughter Andrea from 2012 through late 2016, as well as Paul’s girlfriend in Kyiv, ties to [Thomas Barrack Jr.](https://abdymok.medium.com/paul-and-andriana-5ece7524e592), Alexander Vogt, and so on. I could not force myself to read the whole piece carefully, but this bit caught my attention.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/0a73b641-ec01-4556-a768-108cefca4b1d_597x208.png)  
*Suriya Evans-Pritchard Jayanti served as the US energy, cybersecurity, environment, science, technology and health chief in Kyiv from 2018 to 2020.*

A fresh crop of journalists from around the world, many by way of Moscow, have converged on Kyiv over the last eight months. They are not providing a fresh perspective on the overworked, underburdened experience of US diplomats, preferring to instead specialize on the return of dead American crusaders and unbearable misery of occupation.

The six-minute reportage below caught my eye last week. It’s about a dead volunteer fighter with no prior combat experience who tagged along with the [Norman Brigade](https://twitter.com/BrigadeNormande), a Canadian-led whatever, that raises money on Twitter and Facebook selling war-versus-Russia merchandise. The unit is [reportedly](https://www.theglobeandmail.com/world/article-russia-ukraine-war-foreign-legion/) in the process of being folded into Ukraine’s [notorious](https://kyivindependent.com/investigations/suicide-missions-abuse-physical-threats-international-legion-fighters-speak-out-against-leaderships-misconduct) International Legion.

Apropos of *shifting alliances*, CNN credits two members of Ukraine’s parliament who helped retrieve the corpse — MPs Oleksandr Trukhin (kicked out of the president’s Servant of the People Party for offering a cop a $150,000 bribe after a car wreck) and Aleksandr Kovaliov (the ex-Afghanistan war paratrooper who dresses up in Stalin tunics. He’s standing between MPs Vadym Novitsky and Andriy Derkach, both notorious pro-Russia stooges, in the photo below).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/cf70b7f2-db86-4908-886e-85594d08ed01_1536x2048.jpeg)  
*Photo c/o Alexandr Kovaliov*

Which brings me Charles A. Kupchan’s [scream for attention](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/11/02/opinion/russia-ukraine-negotiation.html?fbclid=IwAR1_6Ci89EbbVr9bOR7yeFyMSiP8UpzRmeFLB3tY4bPJ7UGpoR98ViOG6qA) appearing in The New York Times, titled “**It’s Time to Bring Russia and Ukraine to the Negotiating Table**.” I’m not sure what the latest version of the op-ed is. It’s been revised and republished repeatedly. The paragraph below, however, remains, as of this writing, and that’s bad enough.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/0c6db3a3-c89b-430d-9611-4b8b84f1999b_658x250.jpeg)

From 2014 to 2017, Kupchan served as Special Assistant to the President and Senior Director for European Affairs on the National Security Council in the Obama White House.

Such staying power for a public servant unused outside the realm of parody since, oh, 1997? Shaping Ukraine’s strategic objectives is such a drained cliché, malleable, as was its antonym long before Kupchan smirked into view.

Kupchan was probably out eating lunch when we cleared the air last September about meddling in Ukraine’s affairs[1](#footnote-1). As I recall, he and his deputies were similarly clueless when I chatted them up about Belarus during my former life. As I recall, at the time he was Director for European Affairs on the National Security Council during Bill Clinton's first term. The message then was **do something now** because, otherwise, the Belarusian brand of despotism and inanity will inevitably spread to Russia and Ukraine. Which, of course, it did.

[Setting the Plate Clear](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/setting-the-plate-clear). *Subjectivizing Ukraine and Wagner PMC* (September 6, 2021)

## Lex and Fiona

2022-11-05 · Shadow effect

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/354f5bf0-bbbc-4fff-bec6-5c55653c493a_1024x575.webp)  
*Photo by Zhou Li Ning/EyeEm/Getty Images*

The participants of a popular podcast appear to fart out bricks during a conversation that lasts three hours and nineteen minutes. I listened to it this morning, partly out of a misty nostalgia for the salad days of perestroika, when I still had a sense of humour, but mainly to get a sense about Lex and better understand how a famous Russia expert thinks.

Without saying anything new, Fiona and Lex — both first-generation American citizens — outline in as much detail as possible a worldview and practical guidelines that form Putin’s behavioral model. From their positions, at least, the dialog is entertaining, but not a great tool for studying the genesis and anatomy of the fascistic genocidal campaign I’m in the middle of.

It would have amusing been if they would have/could have had their discussion in Russian, instead of attempting to just demonstrate their ability to speak the language by blurting out a couple of sentences at the start.

Fiona’s urging *sympathetic empathy* for Vova reminded me of the shadow effect. The Russophilic view that only the ability to perceive the world from Putin’s logical and perceptual points of departure — not “bounded rationality” — should no longer be regnant.

**A fun fact.** It turns out Fiona attended [Thorez](https://linguanet.ru/en/) in 1987, the year *after* me. I wonder if she also had to shower with North Korean students in the dorm on Petroverigskiy Pereulok. Perhaps. Maybe not.

We crossed paths briefly when she was director of strategic planning at Eurasia Foundation in Washington, D.C. What a disaster [that](https://www.kyivpost.com/article/content/ukraine-politics/scandal-shakes-eurasia-foundation-280.html) was. Maybe she explains why in her [book](https://books.google.com.ua/books?id=XDQKEAAAQBAJ&printsec=frontcover&dq=fiona+hill&hl=en&sa=X&redir_esc=y#v=onepage&q=fiona%20hill&f=false). Perhaps. I doubt it.

Lex has chatted up lots controversial people, such as Noam Chomsky, Jordan Peterson, Joe Rogan and Oliver Stone, with, er, outrageous views about Russia and Ukraine. In those interviews, he reminds me of a doctor trying to help patients whose violent dreams begin to merge with reality and evolve to mirror disasters they become experts about. Like [Ben Shapiro](https://twitter.com/lexfridman/status/1587112350057889795).

## Day 259

2022-11-08 · Are we dead yet?

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/d3eae30d-3796-45e7-b18d-b97e9d385ecc_1894x1164.jpeg)  
*c/o abdymok*

I wrote and deleted a thousand paragraphs the other day: love letters to impotent rage and monumental sadness. I wanted to get it down, the wet bloodlust, the surge of adrenaline and *bring it on you fuckers you call that obscene.* Before I stood up, phone in hand, and read [this](https://indianexpress.com/article/world/nightlife-darkness-kyiv-ukraine-8246541/):

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/51317f55-4692-45f5-b608-12ac72021e4a_535x447.png)

Kyiv at night is indeed a magic place to discover with feet, à vélo or with horse, but I’ve never heard it described this way. The Club for Aesthetic Disobedience, which for years has criticized “beautification projects” in the capital, describes our architectural jungle the best: a poorly lit dog’s breakfast.

So far in November a [fascinating collection](https://edition.cnn.com/2022/11/02/opinions/ukraine-russia-iran-weapons-geopolitical-ghitis/index.html) of screen captures from online news sources. The urgency and directness of cnn.com analysis has been striking: there’s such pure logic in Clarissa’s stand up. If you can keep your head while others pivot, etc…

And it’s fun to remember how clueless and dotcommified The Washington Post was just a couple of months ago. You can picture some worthless little editor, sweating bullets, asking *was it really 36 days, 28 days or just 14?*

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/656921ac-edba-454a-bc4c-0e471f034aa9_672x288.png)  
*Battle for Kyiv (Washington Post, August 24, 2022)*

I would argue that Putin made his biggest foreign blunder eight years earlier, when he annexed Crimea and invaded eastern Ukraine. Years of botched reporting and historicism obscured that monumental fuck-up, which is today biting us all in the ass.

Oh, and I forgot to mention [Able Archer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Able_Archer_83) in my last post about the marathon conversation hosted by Lex[1](#footnote-1). Fiona cites the imbroglio as an example of a situation that could have ended humanity’s existence on the planet. Actually, no. But this *is* a case where former high-ranking American officials — in this case Fiona — blab about something they have no first-hand knowledge about[2](#footnote-2)[3](#footnote-3).

Another weird interview moment: Fiona mentioned Putin’s willingness to meet Masha Gessen in 2012, understanding this as a sign of his willingness to entertain opposing points of view. Fiona, as I recall, was silent during the [meltdown](https://web.archive.org/web/20121231015020/http://www.worldaffairsjournal.org/blog/judy-bachrach/korn-fired-meltdown-radio-free-europeradio-liberty) of RFE/RL in Moscow after that pow wow.

Masha’s brother Keith – typically unable to establish any sort of authoritative voice before flinging together a few thoughts of comfort to the greedy and the stupid – has [taken the lead](https://www.newyorker.com/culture/annals-of-inquiry/how-the-war-in-ukraine-might-end) in the New Yorker’s steeplechase of hacks making hay out of unspeakable tragedy.

On the Ukraine jacket, there’s not much to report, except Z’s decision yesterday to nationalize everything not nailed down. We’re still somewhere in Phase 3, with less electricity, experiencing an *operational pause* while the warring sides seek to replenish reserves and supply lines as quickly as possible.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a11eb4b9-c194-4018-8981-82829c77eaec_892x291.jpeg)

Stefan provides tactical details about the bloody mess [here](https://www.facebook.com/stefan.korshak).

## Autumn ends

2022-11-10 · If you're walking on eggs don't hop

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/16f6aa53-7310-427e-8006-f453bac953e2_683x400.png)  
*Forecast c/o Gismeteo.ua*

Bicycle season ends when the temperatures dip below zero. I’ve made the 10K commute to/from work in the city for the last five months. Recent rides have become very technical in order to shave off a few seconds here and there. The bike parkour equivalent of the countdown scene in the movie Blue Thunder, according to Google Fit, the [app](https://www.google.com/fit/) that keeps track of everything. It nags me about the twenty-one minute record I need to beat.

Riding fast and fluid through Kyiv can be dangerous. When it comes to braking, I often jump off the bike and slow down on foot, and I also can use my foot on the back wheel like a brake (in an emergency). But usually I try to keep riding.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/b4b68e54-dcb7-4fa1-a902-66e7dc1fa1b3_4395x1491.jpeg)  
*c/o abdymok*

Everyone crashes from time to time, even with breaks. Staying focused and aware of the jackass in your rear-vew mirror is the best way to stay safe.

---

Hypoxia is a life-threatening emergency for fish. An hours-long electricity outage last night killed one of our freshwater angels. Her siblings are sluggish. I read that you can pour hydrogen peroxide in the tank to increase oxygen levels in an emergency, but I’m afraid that might kill the catfish.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/ac95a654-c683-4fd2-af14-bf589ef87d50_600x400.jpeg)  
*A freshwater angelfish*

I am of late – for the first time, and possibly the last – finding myself engaged in the most enjoyable pastime of teaching Ukrainian children how to swear in English. Of course all children come equipped with a threshhold of gullibility, and by any measure it is low, low behaviour for an adult to take advantage of this eagerness to learn, in any language. Nonetheless I hope that some time in the future one of them will have opportunity, when called out or sassed upon an English-speaking street, to shoot back with one of my recommended rejoinders.

***Fuck off and die.***

---

## Melodrama

2022-11-12 · And spin and spin

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/42c8016a-41d2-449e-8908-b6af19275ea7_4177x2439.jpeg)  
*c/o abdymok*

And to all those new Ukraine experts, still blathering on about Putin, you’re doing it wrong. But you knew that.

[The New York Times](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/11/11/world/europe/putin-russia-kherson.html):

“[…] With each new pronounced setback in Ukraine, however, it is getting harder for Mr. Putin to separate himself from the whiff of failure, which is gradually eroding his image as a decisive, indomitable leader. […]”

What?

[The Financial Times](https://www.ft.com/content/396db8b9-87c3-4b31-8b78-3c14ffcba3a7), c/o Henry Foy, the ex-Moscow Bureau Chief famous for [fellating Putin](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FbY0VpyjtuI&ab_channel=FinancialTimes) three years ago, *[analyses](https://www.ft.com/content/396db8b9-87c3-4b31-8b78-3c14ffcba3a7)* the fall of Kherson this week.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/92de2965-22fa-481e-83f7-bf331ae19fef_633x143.png)

Dear Henry! Russia has achieved *nothing* militarily in this almost 9-month campaign. **Rinse, repeat.** And you, Henry, assisted a bloody dictator!

Somewhere in the gaping void of insecurity that permeates dispatches about our unspeakable tragedy there lie previously ignored democracy promotion possibilities. We know flaks employed by NYT and FT haven’t found them yet. Timothy Snyder [@CNN](https://edition.cnn.com/videos/tv/2022/11/11/timothy-snyder-ukraine-midterms-democracy-history.cnn) thinks he has, but we’re not convinced.

## z1 Slow Movement

2022-11-15 · Oligophrens and psychopaths

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/0e8864ec-8716-45cf-868a-004cf591c179_1456x1092.jpeg)

Hitler was hesitant to order the execution of Ernst Röhm, perhaps because of loyalty or embarrassment about executing his WWI buddy, but he relented[1](#footnote-1). On June 30, 1934, hundreds of senior [Sturmabteilung](http://bit.ly/3TAWwD1) officers, including Röhm, were shot dead in the purge known as the Night of Long Knives[2](#footnote-2).

We don’t know yet how the witch hunt in Moscow will unfold or if Viktor Zolotov, the wacko in charge of Putin’s Brownshirts, will be allowed to commit suicide[3](#footnote-3).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/ae423471-f771-42b2-8ed7-bbc7ea6b5613_1024x633.jpeg)  
*Viktor Zolotov (Photo by Ria Novosti)*

It is impossible to predict the behavior of oligophrens and psychopaths. Tiger Team hasn’t come up with the AI that will be able to alert us to the 21st-century remake of [Operation Hummingbird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Night_of_the_Long_Knives), or tell us with any degree of certainty when Russia will erupt.

Maybe this is why Mark Milley told the [Economic Club of New York](https://www.econclubny.org/web/pages/recent-speakers/-/blogs/gen-mark-a-milley) last week said that it’s a good idea, militarily, to lock in gains after battlefield success. (I couldn’t find a transcript of Mark’s comments or a video recording of the event and don’t know whether he was quoted accurately on [this](https://twitter.com/EconClubNY) Twitter thread. The video recording of the event is available only to club members one week *after* the event.)

#### **Well-dressed sexy robots**

Which brings me to the unnamed U.S. officials quoted in an article appearing in Politico on November 14, titled **[U.S. scrambles to reassure Ukraine after Milley comments on negotiations:](https://www.politico.com/news/2022/11/14/u-s-ukraine-milley-negotiations-00066777)** *[The top general's remarks about a “window” for talks angered Ukrainian officials, people familiar with the matter said.](https://www.politico.com/news/2022/11/14/u-s-ukraine-milley-negotiations-00066777)”*

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/44a2ed57-604d-4708-af14-4bc7d3b6c372_827x203.png)

Last time I checked, Ukraine spent eight years talking [about peace talks] before Putin attempted to decapitate Kyiv (again) in February 2022. The anonymous American officials Politico flaks talked with for their *story* are either morons or work for General Berrier at DIA. Or both.

> “The Ukrainian military **will not accept any negotiations, agreements or compromise decisions**. **There is only one condition for negotiations - Russia must leave all captured territories.**” Commander-in-Chief of the Armed Forces of Ukraine, Valeriy Zaluzhny [said](https://t.me/CinCAFU/311?fbclid=IwAR19PyyrHBVLSxFVlYP_px1xdhxb5qc3nLfgbTib83JPyqZVwjJ0pUIEEYQ) in a phone call on November 14, 2022 with Chairman of the U.S. Joint Chiefs of Staff, General Mark Milley.

Later, rinse, repeat.

---

Slow dark blackout days in Kyiv are a great foundation for many things: building a long aerobic base, a huge variety in metabolism daily, since there are three-hour chunks at z1 slow movement, but also some z3 in steep non-technical climbs and even some z4 and z5 in certain zones, if you want to go fast.

Unfortunately, there have been plenty of anerobic enhanced non-divergent stunts engineered by self-aggrandizing doucherockets whose mission in life is to make Z look heroic. The latest is a [hairbrained decision](https://detector.media/community/article/204942/2022-11-14-khersonskyy-ekstses-yak-poiednaty-interesy-suspilstva-zhurnalistiv-vlady-ta-viyskovykh/) to revoke the accreditation of journalists who arrived in liberated Kherson ahead of the *leader*.

## History of Modern Ukraine

2022-11-19 · Classroom lectures

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/fddd96ec-8f9b-4603-86f6-52305a950130_4608x3456.jpeg)  
*Vydubichi. November 19, 2022 (Photo c/o abdymok )*

The title of lesson 19 of Timothy Snyder’s on-line course, “Oligarchies in Ukraine and Russia,” sounded interesting, so I listened to the lecture. Twice. And then I looked at the [syllabus](https://snyder.substack.com/p/syllabus-of-my-ukraine-lecture-class).

Nothing about Murti-Bing pills or the Sino-Mongolian army or Murti-Bingism.

I didn’t learn anything about oligarchies in Russia and Ukraine from this lecture, which was mostly about Polish fantasies (Jerzy Giedroyc, Ivan Rudnytsky) *about* Ukraine pre-1991. [Marci](https://history.yale.edu/people/marci-shore) will talk about Manafort in lesson 20, Tim said.

Whatever.

I think about the mess differently, because my intellectual political baggage is packed with Miłosz, Budinas, Bykov, Taras, Baradullin et al. Last time I checked, Poland’s experiment in state building had devolved into a weird pseudo religious strain of electoral authoritarianism.

Oh, and Tim has teamed up with Z and @U24\_gov\_ua to raise funds to buy [a fleet of naval drones](https://www.navalnews.com/naval-news/2022/11/ukraine-maritime-drone-strikes-again-reports-indicate-attack-on-novorossiysk/). Apropos of nothing, Z yesterday announced at the ***[Bloomberg](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9eF1EuQAps&ab_channel=%D0%9E%D1%84%D1%96%D1%81%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%A3%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%B0%D1%97%D0%BD%D0%B8)*** [New Economy Forum](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9eF1EuQAps&ab_channel=%D0%9E%D1%84%D1%96%D1%81%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%A3%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%B0%D1%97%D0%BD%D0%B8) that there is no corruption in Ukraine.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/87bf2cfe-c340-4c16-ab56-ee40dd4984c2_1024x918.jpeg)  
*Minsk. 1996. Photo by Serhiy Grits*

Here’s a second, more cogent lecture about modern Ukraine by Vitaly Portnikov, who talks to students studying journalism about contemporary myths involving language issues, separatism, idiotarianism, populism, etc.

Unfortunately, if you’re a good journalist in Ukraine, you don't have an Achilles' heel. Because you're just a piece of shit in the middle of the track that eventually is going to get run over by the Z!Team train.

Which reminds me of this [Podereviansky](https://uk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9B%D0%B5%D1%81%D1%8C_%D0%9F%D0%BE%D0%B4%D0%B5%D1%80%D0%B2%27%D1%8F%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%8C%D0%BA%D0%B8%D0%B9) skizzieren:

> Once upon a time there was a rooster and a cat who lived in the forest. The fox wanted to eat the rooster for dinner. This tale follows similar plot, but instead of the rooster and cat, we have two pederasts, Vasyl and Petro.
>
> One day, the two seek refuge in the woods to escape the dreariness of modern existence. Globablism and civilization catch up with them when Mikita – a cannibal – tries to eat Petro for purely ideological reasons. Mikita, you see, had spent his whole life fighting globalism, which, for some unknown reason, he blamed pederasts for.
>
> The story has a happy ending, though. Instead of eating Petro, Mikita becomes a pederast, too. And in so doing, good conquers evil, etc.

## The Captive Mind

2022-11-20 · IX: The lesson of the Baltics

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/d185588e-4186-48c1-85f7-aa910021d365_1440x810.jpeg)  
*Marta, Lviv, August 2022.(Photo by Chien-Chi Chang )*

When *someone is honestly 55% right, that's very good and there's no wrangling. And if someone is 60% right it's wonderful, it's great luck, and let him thank God. But what's to be said about 75% right? Wise people say this is suspicious. Well, and what about 100% right? Whoever says he's 100% right is a fanatic, a thug, and the worst kind of rascal.* - an old Jew of Galacia

The parable to the preface of [The Captive Mind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Captive_Mind) by Czesław Miłosz.

---

It reminds me of Z, who [told us](https://www.reuters.com/world/europe/ukraines-president-says-ukrainian-missile-did-not-cause-blast-poland-agency-2022-11-16/) last week that he had no doubt that an explosion that killed two people in Poland was not caused by a Ukrainian missile and [said](https://menafn.com/1105206992/Zelensky-claims-to-have-rid-Ukraine-of-corruption) Ukraine is corruption free.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a2a75c1a-0cb8-4bfe-9e1d-d31e440596ba_347x543.png)  
*Books to borrow. Cover c/o archive.org*

The last chapter, IX, is about my room mate from Riga in Moscow, Edik, who enjoyed fighting with his elbows, energized by [The Origins of Totalitarianism](https://archive.org/details/originsoftotalit0000unse_j3v9/page/n5/mode/2up).

Which brings me to this paragraph of an op-ed by David in The Washington Post, titled “[The U.S. seeks to support Ukraine, but contain the war.](https://www.washingtonpost.com/opinions/2022/11/18/ukraine-war-william-burns-russia-poland-missile/)”

> Third, let’s think about the delicate relationship between Washington and Kyiv. Zelensky has the power of a brave, charismatic leader to pressure his superpower patron into actions that might not be in the United States’ interests. The Biden administration has tried to strike a balance between strong military support for Ukraine and avoiding anything that might trigger a direct Russian-American conflict.

Brave, charismatic? Maybe desperate? What does “direct conflict” mean? And no, sorry, William is *not* fluent in Russian. He knows the alphabet and dozens of words. His pronunciation is lousy.

Looking on the positive side, Lloyd in Hallifax [says](https://www.defense.gov/News/News-Stories/Article/Article/3224096/austin-says-ukraine-matters-to-all-peoples-and-nations/) Team USA *is* meeting the urgent, historic security challenge Russia’s tyranny poses to the rules-based international order.

Wonderful. Let’s just hope we aren’t irradiated and freeze to death during the process.

## Pugnacious Individualism

2022-11-24 · Ukraine's trolley problem

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/a916267e-6945-48d9-82e4-1786f5d547b5_680x378.webp)

So, [Penguin Random House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penguin_Random_House) over the summer proposed itself as the only publisher of the condensed (or large-print) version of the *ur*-text of approved pep rhetoric. [A Message from Ukraine](https://www.amazon.com/Message-Ukraine-Speeches-2019-2022-ebook/dp/B0BGX2HNJ9) contains a preface that explains the context of the incised line breaks and verse settings.

That brings us to George Will and his Tim Snyderish op-ed urging us to [Be Thankful for Zelensky’s Magnificent Resistance to Suppression](https://www.washingtonpost.com/opinions/2022/11/23/zelensky-resistance-ukraine-war/).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/0dd4ced1-1915-4c6d-b8d4-a70ea653e36d_667x261.png)

Of course, there’s a fine line between pugnacious individualism and stupidity, historicism and historiography, college lectures[1](#footnote-1) and reality, et cetera. It would be churlish of me to wade into that mess now because the water has been turned off and I wouldn’t be able to take a long, hot shower afterwards.

**For the history books.**

[According to former British Prime Minister Boris Johnson](https://edition.cnn.com/2022/11/22/europe/boris-johnson-ukraine-invasion-europe-comments-intl/index.html), in the run-up to Russia’s all-out invasion of Ukraine on February 24, 2022:

- UK leaders were in denial.
- Ukraine’s leaders were in denial.
- France’s leaders were in denial.
- Germany, under the assumption the war would happen, cynically hoped for a rapid Russian victory for economic reasons.
- Italy's initial position was to make zero sacrifices.

Meanwhile, jackasses at the U.S. Defense Intelligence Agency led by Lieutenant General Scott Berrier [believed](https://www.military.com/daily-news/2022/03/10/us-misjudged-ukraines-will-fight-russia-officials-admit.html) Kyiv would be overrun in 72 hours.

The “war” has since then turned out to be less than an exercise in power balancing against an external threat than an ongoing operation of mass murder[2](#footnote-2). Leaders in Europe and the United State don’t want to admit this publicly, yet, because it would increase pressure on them to intervene in order to stop more needless suffering and senseless killing.

We have discussed stay-behind sabotage[3](#footnote-3) and the Third A[4](#footnote-4)[5](#footnote-5) several times.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/987173f1-8344-4175-bcef-4536707a03f3_1280x1176.jpeg)  
*c/o AFU*

War is not unlike a badly-trained lion in the circus that doesn’t obey the people who let it out of its cage.

In other news, the other day I watched this 37-minute chit chat between Frishman and Gessen broadcast by TV Rain. So you don’t have to.

It is almost 20 years since Masha teamed up with Steven Korn, a former CNN executive, to fuel the meltdown of RFE/RL’s Russia bureau. Masha, who had written a critical book on Putin, met with the Russian autocrat shortly before she took over the bureau — a horrible misstep. She only lasted a couple of months. She and Korn were sacked [after](https://abdymok.blogspot.com/2013/01/sergei-kovalevs-on-rferl-disgrace-nov-4.html) doing enormous damage. Now she’s a Ukraine expert, teaches jornoism and inserts commas at [The New Yorker](https://www.newyorker.com/contributors/masha-gessen), which pretends to ignore the RFE/RL disaster.

## Practical Homicide Investigation

2022-11-26 · Ukraine's counterfactual scad

Dialogical hooey — nodal points of history and contrastive explanations — is an annoying feature of Russia’s botched invasion of Ukraine. Thoughts about how this bloody mess might have turned out differently are known to psychologists as counterfactual thinking, or *un*-thinking, as it were.

An example:

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/98dddb84-4ebd-4686-a808-60d5b68f9b06_541x442.jpeg)  
*Putin’s Likely Course of Action in Ukraine (ISW, December 2021)*

Today the ISW team, along with the [UK’s Ministry of Defence](https://twitter.com/DefenceHQ), churn out daily briefs about the event they told us was “highly unlikely” to happen. [Ukraine invasion updates](https://www.criticalthreats.org/) are the bread and butter of Fredrick W. Kagan, who [argues](https://www.criticalthreats.org/analysis/the-case-against-negotiations-with-russia) that negotiations with Russia are futile.

I attempted to read Fred’s 1,400-word *analysis*, titled “[The Case Against Negotiations with Russia](https://www.criticalthreats.org/analysis/the-case-against-negotiations-with-russia),” without much success.

But this passage jumped out at me.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/ab32250d-d97a-4595-ad3e-fe288117e65e_606x690.png)

When it comes to written news reports, a corpus analysis (n ≈ 1 zillion words) reveals that Russia-invasion news reports contain startlingly high proportions of certain difficult-to-process impenetrable features – including extra hard-to-pronounce letter combinations and weird-looking first- and last name suffixes relative to baseline genres of written and spoken English.[1](#footnote-1)

Fred’s fears about Ukraine *becoming* “a permanent economic basket case” are passé because the country has been one for, er, the last 30 years. Maybe *remaining* would be a better word to use. Kudos, though, for spelling Zaporizhia correctly.

[1](#footnote-anchor-1)

[Day 10](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/day-10). *Simplifying public-facing nouns* (March 5, 2022)

*Andy chats up Z on NYT’s Dealbook on November 30, 2022*

Z invites Elon to Kyiv. Ugh.

Maybe [The Operator](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/sovereign-state) can arrange the visit. Sean Penn can accompany. With [Imagine Dragons](https://www.instagram.com/p/CgVGLfCvlC3/). They can all sing [Carol of the Bells](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EKkzbbLYPuI) after Klitschko erects the [12-meter tall diesel-generator powered Xmas tree](https://www.reuters.com/world/kyiv-s-mayor-we-cannot-allow-putin-steal-our-christmas-2022-11-29/) next week.

Some time ago, before Putin tried to decapitate Kyiv, Ukraine [announced](https://mediasat.info/ru/2021/02/03/igor-yaremchuk-ukrtelecom/) that every community with a hundred potential broadband subscribers would have ADSL brought to them. Months later my local *mairie* in Semihory snapped into action by putting a petition on its reception desk. But in a village where the median age is about seventy, and where there are probably more hatchets than computers, and where no one ever goes to the *селсовет,* there was no mad rush to sign up. So I had to do it myself.

For years I tried to get satellite to work, but the rates were always ridiculous (consumption-wise, analogous to a hundred bucks for a bowl of tapwater), and while my Fijitsu could theoretically be configured to negotiate the VPN moat and drawbridge of satellite broadband, people were happy to point out that no one ever had.

After Kyivstar erected a tower up the road, though, rates became more affordable. Thanks to Elon, I pay nothing for my Starlink connection. Packages are on offer that compete in price with terrestrial DSL, without miserly bandwidth limitations. So I just signed the hell up, using a false name and fake address. My connection is a lot faster than the retired Russian general with the pig and goat cheese farm two *хутори* away.

My two Twitter accounts are [abdymok](https://twitter.com/abdymok) and [dronedept](https://twitter.com/dronedept). I use them to keep track of [bodega cats](https://twitter.com/Bodegacats_) and Ukraine experts, such as [Timothy Snyder](https://twitter.com/TimothyDSnyder), who is finding it enormously difficult to put together a lecture about Ukraine’s contemporary political history post Independence.


---

## Regulated Shamanic Reforms

2022-12-14 · Ayn Rand kamikaze drones

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/6b285379-95ad-4527-a766-126772af0aae_1080x542.jpeg)  
*Time change rationale (c/o abdymok )*

Not much to report. Deoccupation proceeds, albeit at a slower pace because of the mud. Meanwhile, attacks on Russia’s center of gravity show an expanded capability of Ukraine’s long-range assets of destruction (Engels, Ryazan, Kursk, Bryansk, etc). And Uncle Joe has agreed, [reportedly](https://edition.cnn.com/2022/12/13/politics/us-patriot-missile-defense-system-ukraine/index.html), to upgrade Ukraine’s air defenses, eventually.

I listened to Timothy’s latest and last lecture about the Making of Modern Ukraine. He leaves a lot out.

The final class is not about unfettered muddling and unclarity during the 2000s, 2010s and 2020s. Tim glosses over [Tuzla](https://www.kyivpost.com/article/content/ukraine-politics/tense-over-tuzla-19153.html), post-Orange Revolution idiocy leading to the election of a Kremlin goon, Kharkiv accords and the lead-up to Russia’s invasion in 2014. The authoritarian populist accelerant to Putin’s decision to decapitate Kyiv is ignored.

Pre-war Ukraine should be remembered as a sham democracy, a primitive post-Soviet feudal autocracy not underpinned by elections or coordinated work of institutions, but by popular uprisings ([2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_without_Kuchma), [2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange_Revolution), [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolution_of_Dignity)). Absent them, even the illusion of tolerance, much less competency, would not exist. What happens after we win the war is anyone’s guess. Should we embrace pan European socialist-styled remedies? Or should we emulate Ireland?

Which brings me to the non-existent seven-foot tall monument of Ronald Reagan in Kyiv, c/o Jeff Stein at [The Washington Post](https://edition.cnn.com/2022/12/13/politics/us-patriot-missile-defense-system-ukraine/index.html).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/dd93652e-994e-43e0-b00e-726c13f647a9_684x200.png)

Ukraine’s Ayn Rand cult reminded me of this C-Span chit chat between Christopher Hitchens and David Frum 17 years ago.

The article about Zablotskyy’s brainchild followed a long, loopy report, titled, “[War has tamed Ukraine’s oligarchs, creating space for democratic change:](https://www.washingtonpost.com/world/2022/12/08/ukraine-oligarchs-power-war/) *[Ukraine may have the opportunity to rebuild a postwar society that is more democratic, less corrupt and more economically diversified](https://www.washingtonpost.com/world/2022/12/08/ukraine-oligarchs-power-war/)*[.](https://www.washingtonpost.com/world/2022/12/08/ukraine-oligarchs-power-war/)”

Those interviewed include economist Timothy Mylovanov, president of the Kyiv School of Economics and a former government economic development minister, who said Ukrainians’ economic future would follow a U.S. or European model, not a Russian one.

More lawyers and less bribers, concluded Mylovanov, one of the most active and self-aware villians of Ukraine’s macroeconomic mess under Z.

But that’s another story.

## Day 295

2022-12-15 · A big stupid circle

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/3ae36964-736d-4fe5-aa53-15dbd495ddc3_1035x591.jpeg)  
*Photo by Herbert Villarraga (Reuters)*

*[Wash trading](https://www.investopedia.com/terms/w/washtrading.asp)* is a strategy in which the buyer and the seller are effectively on both sides of the deal, and a person will essentially sell themselves an asset to create the illusion that a particular asset is far more attractive than it actually is.

Imagine you're a pop star signed to a major label who has produced a new song. You really, really want to get it out to a top hits playlist because people who listen to music often use them as a guide to what they should listen to next.

So to do this, your label identifies hardcore fans, lures them to your concerts and fills up Internet with crap that reminds people of you. If your songs are any good, you rise on the charts. And then fans want to hear and see more.

The same is true for self-help books sold on Amazon. The practice can even apply to pilgrimages by visiting dignitaries to Bucha outside of Kyiv, which is what I thought when I listened to Serhiy Leshchenko [explain](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CeCPBPN7-yo) laboriously to Mark Feygin why Z has been recognized *correctly* as Person of the Year.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/04a25390-7c8a-436d-9b28-16fb43bd793f_800x1066.webp)  
*Illustration c/o Time*

We must not be distracted by the circular logic of needy operators.

Pop rock stars[1](#footnote-1), Hollywood actors[2](#footnote-2) and presidential pep talks[3](#footnote-3) win fame, not wars. Just like a year ago, when Z was insisting an all-out invasion was unlikely[4](#footnote-4), what’s necessary today is a trained, healthy and fully-equipped fighting force capable of vaporizing the enemy.

Before the February 2022, the outside world lived in fantasy land about Ukraine and Z because of loopy reporting. No one was *that* interested in Kyiv until the blitzkrieg. World leaders have only just recently showed signs of coming to their senses.

A lot of people have asked whether Z and his followers can be convinced to reach an accommodation with Vova in order to end the bloodletting. The answer is no for a zillion reasons. Russia’s military forces in Ukraine must be defeated and driven out. It’s a simple as that. The invaders must die. Rinse, repeat.

Think [JDAM strikes](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o5bH7VurGTs&ab_channel=mattezyk69).

## Day 296

2022-12-16 · A big bucket of sand

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/0411d263-6e5c-448e-9535-395af12606f0_840x1132.jpeg)  
*Illustration by Mikhail Aleksandrov*

[The Economist](http://economist.com) dug the black hole of Ukraine-related bullshit a little deeper this week.

> **Officials** close to Mr Zelensky are quick to point out that General Zaluzhny has not visited the front line. That, say the general’s defenders, is because the same officials have barred him from doing so. Some in the president’s circle would like to replace him with General Oleksandr Syrsky, who commands Ukraine’s land forces, over whose head General Zaluzhny was promoted last year.[1](#footnote-1)

**Officials? Advisors to officials? Operators?** Maybe whoever wrote this bullshit doesn’t understand the difference.

The passage reminds of when one-time welfare recipient Diane Francis foamed for the [Kyiv Post](https://kyivpost.com) about Andrey Illarionov’s imagined Kremlin/Washington disinformation campaign to create a full-blown propagandized invasion threat one month before the blitzkrieg.[2](#footnote-2)

This brings me back to the main conclusion of yesterday’s post[3](#footnote-3):

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/2e8d3fbb-0f44-4af9-bd42-7046daaf06b5_661x136.png)

Non-permissive environment specialists have told me that it’s okay to carouse with the greasy clowns at [Tootsie Club](https://en.tootsie.kiev.ua/), but **a very bad idea** to regurgitate their bullshit in print, much less take it seriously. Leave that to Diane.

---

In other news, Christmas trees have gone on sale at [dozens of locations](https://m.facebook.com/kyivbusinesscapital/posts/pfbid0WpQLcbSA7ng1rMf2q6AoaKSiiHyYan2fQH4AEy1DFVBSmGreucP84wJd3T8LwMi9l) in the capital. Unfortunately, [tree stands](https://www.nytimes.com/wirecutter/reviews/best-christmas-tree-stand/) are in short supply, so this year we might have to resort to a big bucket and sand from the banks of the mighty Dnipro.

## Day 297

2022-12-17 · Dead white male expat monkeys

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/7c18678c-7704-4a16-a828-7f1bc71a449e_840x1132.jpeg)  
*Illustration by Mikhail Aleksandrov*

[Headlines](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/12/16/world/europe/ukraine-russia-missiles-infrastructure.html) of reports in foreign media about residents of Ukraine’s capital freezing to death after yesterday’s Russia’s missile attack sound a lot worse than how I feel now, this morning, the day after.

Dead white male expat monkeys in Kyiv are stanning themselves, posting screams. I am amused, then annoyed.

Same goes for dead white male Ukraine experts abroad.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/87597ca7-57ad-45e5-9d0a-2408706fbfd4_600x561.png)  
*Timothy’s Twitter*

Who is in charge of the United24 campaign? Why would anyone be so foolish to do what it asks them to?

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/e69396ea-19aa-485f-a1e8-51a3ba1cf44d_592x473.png)  
*Michael’s Twitter*

One thing I fear is people taking McFaul seriously about *anything* relating to fascistic Russia’s genocidal campaign in Ukraine.

Same goes for the bar stools employed by The Washington Post.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/9d4b4828-1a6e-4553-8098-cfcbf5820ef8_630x172.png)  
*Ukraine air defenses counter Russian barrage, but missiles hit energy grid (David L. Stern, The Washington Post, December 16, 2022)*

What the fuck does “generally impossible” mean?

---

In related news:

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/43cce8e0-aea9-404e-9e06-da02e20e87de_1372x1600.jpeg)  
*c/o Ruslan Lobanov*

There is nothing deliberately invented in this year’s cycle of works, titled “On the occasion of the death of Putin.”

We see beautiful people celebrating not so much the death of a dictator as the death of the very concept of totalitarianism. A simple and clear idea can be traced in Lobanov’s photographs. Instead of ephemeral denazification, we witness the total identification of citizens of a nation.

The most beautiful nation in the world!

The inability to let go of the past means there is little room left for the present. Instead of the present - a collection of unfinished stories and unnecessary things - we experience the desire to return home after extricating ourselves from the bloody mess and mountains of slush we have been mired in for so long.

In other words, art lives longer than dead white male expat monkeys.

## Day 300ish

2022-12-20 · Inside the wire

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/5773b4a5-ec5b-4ad8-a795-2d178b55e554_3066x4520.jpeg)  
*c/o abdymok*

One passage of the 15,000-word interactive whatever published by The New York Times last week jumped out at me[1](#footnote-1):

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/bdda295e-6a7e-476f-8b78-ee8283aae3d1_632x337.png)

Burns and non-permissive environment specialists later visited Kyiv and schemed with indigenous commandos at [Tootsie Club](https://en.tootsie.kiev.ua/) about possible plans to liberate future oppressed residents of the Ukraine’s capital. The plans were not made public[2](#footnote-2). Two weeks later, the US State Department told Americans to get out of the country immediately[3](#footnote-3).

Which leaves us living out the last stanzas of [Inside the Wire](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tCxheyDT3BE), a cult song by [Bolt Cutter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolt_Thrower), an English death metal band from Coventry.

[…]

*Exist longer under threat  
Engaging to replay the debt  
Damage to infliction  
Patterns lie half formed  
Assaults never retracted  
In the hope to halt the war*

*Laying waste, subversion  
Rendering in chaos with dead ammunition*

*Undermine defenses - facilitate  
Saboteurs inside the wire - interpenetrate  
Inner defense - eradicate  
Inside the wire - too late?*

*Too late*

## Join my chat

2022-12-20 · A private space for us to converse and connect

Today I’m announcing a brand new addition to my Substack publication: the ukraine@war subscriber chat.

This is a conversation space in the Substack app that I set up exclusively for my subscribers — kind of like a group chat or live hangout. If you have questions about fascistic Russia’s genocidal campaign in Ukraine, running routes in Kyiv, organic farming in Semihory, or about what happens to neighboring Belarus after Putin’s regime disintegrates, just ask.

**To join our chat, you’ll need to download the [Substack app](https://substack.com/app/app-store-redirect), now available for both iOS and Android.** Chats are sent via the app, not email, so turn on push notifications so you don’t miss conversation as it happens.

## Day 303

2022-12-23 · The porosity of recombinants

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/48c8d776-ec03-4113-9f1d-46e7a57c90b0_1080x1440.jpeg)  
*c/o abdymok*

Elaborately hung over from slapstick self-anaesthesia, and reveling somehow in the sheer size of my ear pain, I walked without galoshes in a pair of UAH 2,000 Salomon Evasion LTRs bought on [Shafa](http://shafa.ua) via Rivne, squinting in the dark at poorly lit residential complexes, as my legs whisked me down the hill to the heart of democracy.

All five senses get serviced inside the wire. You tell me which is worse: the dry brittle snapping thin ice covering dirty puddles, or the fumes of diesel generators, the sight of overflowing rubbish bins, the super-concentrated unpleasant taste of op-eds published across the lake?

It’s the latter, hands down.

They do [this](https://www.nytimes.com/2022/12/22/opinion/biden-zelensky-speech.html), this is all they do, all they do is this:

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/028ff55b-9401-452f-9c22-bcc9a0ff7f6a_660x261.png)  
*Biden’s America Finds Its Voice. David Brooks, The New York Times (December 22, 2022)*

Regular expression syntax: all other language lies weeping in its wake.

If you’re as bored as I am with the stream of retrograde newspeak from power-dazzled Ukraine experts – those who, despite having long abused the gravitas of instability brought about by unspeakable shortsightedness, continue to use it to justify regurgitating feel-good bullshit (it’s amusing to note that the most lurid post-Cold War fantasies of covert propaganda spread on behalf of Team Democracy by government operatives have been rendered irrelevant by a chattering horde of [useful idiots](https://www.kyivpost.com/author/diane-francis) now serving it up for free every day: who needs black ops when you’ve got Sub\*stack) – then you won’t find a breathtaking voice of reason in David Ignatius[1](#footnote-1).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/4bb3073c-199b-4558-afbf-09fe6c8dfdef_647x241.png)  
*Where Zelensky and Biden still diverge. David Ignatius, The Washington Post (December 22, 2022)*

Which brings me to the war jacket. The Armed Forces of Ukraine [say](https://www.mil.gov.ua/en/news/2022/12/22/the-total-combat-losses-of-the-enemy-from-24-02-to-22-12/) more than 100,000 Russian invaders have been vaporized. The AFU during the last several months has de-occupied about half of the areas seized since February, not including Crimea, accounting for about 20-25% of the country.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/144377c6-674e-4a1f-bce0-9e806f7f217d_1009x625.jpeg)

Stefan chronicles tactical details, military trivia about the bloody mess [here](https://stefankorshak.substack.com/).

##### Predictions for 2023

FEBRUARY A woman will respond to an anecdote told by her boyfriend with unexpected fury, yelling that no, it’s not ‘amazing,’ it’s not even all that ‘significant,’ and that no, it’s not ‘ironic,’ it’s more like a ‘coincidence,’ because his mother is Russian.

MARCH Two office coworkers will conspicuously cease their constant flirting and suddenly avoid eye contact, leading everyone to conclude they’d had an affair and, in the words of their boss, ‘fucked it out of each other.’

JUNE In a Kyiv coffee shop on Reitarska Street, a woman with a BA in English will admit her affection for [Andriy Kurkov,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Kurkov) causing her companion, whom she will just have met after a lengthy exchange via whatsapp, to glaze over and think about Saperavi, which he will consume alone.

JULY A man, his head swimming with Ativaks ear drops, will decide that this, *this right here*, is the best shawarma of all time.

AUGUST Two people, heading back to their offices on Bankova following a brainstorming session with top USAID officials (in town for two days from Washington, D.C.), will shake their heads after one of them says, ‘What a bunch of jerks.’

OCTOBER A child, having his freshly skinned knees cleansed and bandaged, will respond with confused silence when his mother demands, ‘Why do you do this to me?’

DECEMBER Someone at Ukraine’s Ministry of Digital Transformation will print out an email and retype its contents into a Word document.

## The Empty Chair Technique

2022-12-28 · Supplements, sex and magnitism

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/b49862d1-7ef9-4e43-ab72-0850ac6e76bb_1920x1301.png)  
*Photo c/o abdymok*

For Z, who takes pride in his abs, magnitism and the resilience of his constituents, the rubber might meet the road, eventually. But forced conscription quotas and deadlines won’t be the reason. So, we can forget about [our new ambassador to Bulgaria](https://censor.net/ru/resonance/3389617/strategchniyi_proval_u_bolgar_chogo_ne_zrozumv_ofs_prezidenta_priznachayuchi_pan_laschuk), [power lifting in Dnipro](https://www.pravda.com.ua/rus/news/2022/12/26/7382408/) and Rule of Law *per se,* at least until the war ends.

This is why my eyes glazed over reading the nut graph of an article, titled “[Ukraine takes two steps forward, one step back in anti-corruption fight](https://www.politico.eu/article/ukraine-takes-two-steps-forward-one-step-back-in-anti-corruption-fight-constitutional-court-reform/),” appearing in [Politico](https://www.politico.eu/) on December 26.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/c6c80881-92cc-40ed-8f84-d9eb555b081e_800x257.png)

**Face the facts.** No one in Ukraine, including Z, cares what the [Venice Commission](https://www.venice.coe.int/webforms/events/) thinks about anything, there is no momentum on the institution-building front and the French president’s opinion is irrelevant.

Z’s three-ring circus approach to everything might be funny if one didn’t consider the enormous costs of a system of governmance that remains rotten to its core. As a consequence, traitors roam the streets of Vienna, billions of taxpayers’ dollars are wasted with impunity and the Pork Chop from Obolon remains at large[1](#footnote-1).

Ukraine’s judges, law enforcers and lawmakers have not only proven themselves incapable of rooting out corruption – they remain, in fact, the architects of the rancid system in place.

Supplements, sex and magnitism won’t rectify the situation[2](#footnote-2).

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/c607bb37-d165-4c21-a4d8-921dec61f163_705x395.jpeg)  
*Photo c/o Ukrainska Pravda*

In contravention of the Constitution, today most or all of the governing power resides in a centralized government run from the President’s Office. The country is a *Gestalt,* a unified whole that is more than the sum of its parts.

In psychotherapy, *The Empty Chair Technique* originating in *Gestalt Therapy* provides for the client to conduct an emotional dialogue with some aspect of himself or herself or some significant person (e.g., the president), who is imagined to be sitting in an empty chair during the session.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/c20a2354-0a70-4a18-b152-a0166cc5c664_526x526.png)

Just ask Olesia Ilaschuk, Ukraine’s new ambassador to Bulgaria. She believes we should focus on the totality of Z’s functioning and relationships in the [here and now](https://dictionary.apa.org/here-and-now), rather than dwelling on air-raid alerts and past political screw ups, including [Operation Ы](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/operation-y-b2d557374ca7), [Wagnergate](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/drones-in-my-bones), [Chausgate](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/one-more-big-mistake), [Semenchenkogate](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2Opyqyz3xd4&ab_channel=PreZeDentTV), ad nauseum.

[1](#footnote-anchor-1)

[A Man in Full.](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/a-man-in-full) *The Pork Chop from Obolon* (September 10, 2021)

[2](#footnote-anchor-2)

[Supplements, Sex and Magnitism](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zH_Y2MyJoYg&t=111&ab_channel=RainBurdakov) (2021, Vydubichi)

## Over the Top

2022-12-29 · Z's brain might be our worst enemy

Z’s defiant and uplifting oratory has already been chopped up and posted without much challenge in English and American newspapers, ezines and posted to TikTok.

With all due respect, his 45-minute performance in parliament yesterday was *over the top*.

The president said that Ukraine has changed the world, returned to it true values, has become one of the strongest and most free countries in the world. And that we are on the path to victory, thanks to our soldiers, our partners who give weapons, our people who pay taxes and thanks to our internal unity, which should change the traditions of Ukraine’s history of mistakes.

Invited by leaders of the United States and Europe to contribute something, anything, to Ukraine’s military defense, Z proclaimed: “Today NATO standards *are* being determined in Ukraine.” In anyone else this would be a solipsism, rather than one more delusion commingled with truth.

Outside parliament, the Z cult in Ukraine is mild and reflective in comparison with the president’s cheerleaders abroad. The aftermath of February 24 has only reinforced a series of tropes and laid the groundwork for the president’s own plaudits. The rest of the world might wish that if Ukraine is going to stand for something, it (or its overpaid speechwriting class) would try to come up with more original — less Churchillian — motivational rhetoric.

In other news, Ukraine’s newly appointed ambassador to Bulgaria [has told us](https://t.me/olesya_ilashchuk) to ignore hyped-up insinuations about government incompetency and her libido-boosting supplements career.

> Для друзів, читачів каналу і для тих недолугих "журналістів," хто в цей скрутний час намагається "хайпувати" на домислах, ставить під сумнів компетентність рішення уряду та нашого президента і влаштовує безладдя в інтернеті я даю відповідь про свою біографію. Ті, хто пише статті без доказів та перекручує факти на свій лад, розхитує наше суспільство під час воєнного стану— працює на ворога.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/17c436cd-5641-4160-8191-5f96b906b905_640x640.jpeg)

Whatever. Maybe Olesia can sit down for a couple of interviews in order to clear up the mystery about her qualifications for the job.

![](https://bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/6d840798-d53e-492f-86ca-6e22122e2195_960x806.jpeg)
